# Χρήσιμα αποσπάσματα

> Generated αρχείο από τα επαληθευμένα `evidence/SRC-*.md`.
> Περιλαμβάνονται μόνο αρχεία με `κατάσταση: επαληθευμένο` και
> `ελεγχθέν-πρωτότυπο: ναι`. Πρόχειρες επιλογές NotebookLM δεν είναι citation-ready evidence.

- **Πηγές με επαληθευμένα αποσπάσματα:** 113
- **Ιχνηλασιμότητα:** κωδικός `SRC-*`, ακριβής θέση, ισχυρισμός, συμφραζόμενα και περιορισμοί.

## `SRC-01BBBA7EAB` — Robust Reinforcement Learning in POMDPs with Incomplete and Noisy Observations

- **Συγγραφείς:** Yuhui Wang; Hao He; Xiaoyang Tan
- **Έτος:** 2019
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** εύρωστη ενισχυτική μάθηση; μερική παρατηρησιμότητα; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-03
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-01BBBA7EAB.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-01BBBA7EAB.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.05795)

## E1 — The target failure mode is dynamic missingness plus observation noise
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1
- **Claim:** The paper studies RL when observation components can be missing at changing time steps and available sensor values are noisy.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The authors argue that real systems may lose sensor components because of malfunction, preprocessing delays, or asynchronous sampling while the remaining measurements are noisy. This setting violates the complete-observation assumptions used by many continuous-control RL methods. The missing dimensions and their timing are not assumed to be known in advance, so the problem is represented through partial observability rather than a fixed masked input.

## E2 — Decisions are made from a propagated belief state
- **Location:** Sections 3.2 and 4.1, belief-state propagation
- **Claim:** BI-PPO maintains a posterior belief over latent state using the history of incomplete/noisy observations and actions, and the policy acts from that belief representation.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The method propagates an intermediate belief through a learned transition model and then updates it with the available observation components. Missing values are handled within the belief update instead of being treated as ordinary zeros or copied blindly from the previous time step. This is a principled example of resilience to degraded sensing through state estimation, not through modifying the environment transition model itself.

## E3 — The derivation has explicit missingness and distributional assumptions
- **Location:** Section 3.2; Section 4.1
- **Claim:** The method assumes MCAR or MAR missingness and uses Gaussian approximations for tractable belief inference.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The observation indicator is assumed independent of the latent state in the sense required by MCAR/MAR formulations. Noise is modeled with a Gaussian distribution, and the transition/belief calculations use Gaussian or local approximations. These assumptions matter when interpreting robustness results: performance under arbitrary adversarial corruption is not established by this experiment.

## E4 — Observation robustness and environment adaptation are distinct
- **Location:** Abstract; experiments; overall formulation
- **Claim:** The reported robustness concerns corrupted or missing observations, not explicit detection and recovery from a changed MDP.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The experiments vary incompleteness and noise while the agent uses belief imputation to continue executing the task. The paper does not introduce a changepoint detector, context-switching policy memory, or recovery-time metric for structural environment changes. It therefore supports an observation-robustness axis in the thesis and should not be cited as evidence for online non-stationary adaptation.

---

## `SRC-0406E13B97` — Safe Continual Reinforcement Learning Methods for Nonstationary Environments. Towards a Survey of the State of the Art

- **Συγγραφείς:** Timofey Tomashevskiy
- **Έτος:** 2026
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** μη στασιμότητα; ασφαλής ενισχυτική μάθηση; μερική παρατηρησιμότητα; συνεχής προσαρμογή; πράκτορες τεχνητής νοημοσύνης
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-0406E13B97.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-0406E13B97.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05152)

## Evidence E1 — Safe adaptation can require both performance and safety mechanisms to remain valid after change
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1
- **Claim:** Non-stationarity can degrade task performance and also undermine safety assumptions or constraints that were designed for a stationary environment.
- **Thesis use:** safety/performance separation
- **Topics:** safe adaptation; changing constraints; non-stationarity
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
A safe RL method deployed in a changing environment may need to adapt not only the policy or learning mechanism but also the way safety constraints are interpreted or parameterized. An initially adequate safety rule can become ineffective after the environment changes, so performance recovery alone is insufficient evidence of safe continual adaptation.

### Limitation
This does not mean every thesis experiment needs adaptive constraints. If safety rules are fixed by design, the experiment should state that explicitly and measure whether they remain satisfied after the shift.

## Evidence E2 — Non-stationarity should be identified by the MDP/CMDP component that changes and by its origin
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 2.3–2.4
- **Claim:** The survey distinguishes exogenous, endogenous, and hybrid non-stationarity and discusses changes to reward, transition dynamics, action distributions/spaces, and constraints.
- **Thesis use:** shift taxonomy
- **Topics:** passive non-stationarity; active non-stationarity; reward shift; dynamics shift
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The term non-stationarity covers several different mechanisms. A change can be caused by external factors, by the learning agent's own behavior, or by both, and it can affect different parts of the decision process. Experiments should therefore state which component changes instead of using a generic “dynamic environment” label.

## Evidence E3 — Recovery speed and transient safety are separate requirements
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3, problem formulation and adaptation discussion
- **Claim:** Continual safe RL must consider how quickly performance recovers and whether safety constraints remain satisfied during the adaptation interval.
- **Thesis use:** recovery and safety metrics
- **Topics:** adaptation time; transient safety; constraint violations
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Fast return to acceptable task performance is only one dimension of safe adaptation. The agent can recover reward quickly while violating constraints during the transient period, so a safe evaluation should pair recovery-time measures with violation counts, cumulative safety cost, or other declared constraint metrics over the same interval.

## Evidence E4 — Adaptation approaches differ by when and how they respond to non-stationarity
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** method taxonomy in Sections 5–6
- **Claim:** The surveyed methods include passive, reactive, rapid-adaptation, and proactive responses to changing environments.
- **Thesis use:** related-work organization
- **Topics:** reactive adaptation; proactive adaptation; safety layer
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Some approaches remain inside known safe regions without explicitly reacting to a detected change; others update after a change is observed, use meta/context mechanisms for rapid adaptation, or try to anticipate future conditions. These categories encode different information assumptions and should not be ranked as if they solved the same problem under the same oracle access.

## Evidence E5 — Safety-constraint formulations differ in what is allowed to change
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 7 and constraint taxonomy
- **Claim:** Constraints can be fixed, partially adjustable, or adjustable with respect to time or data.
- **Thesis use:** safety formulation disclosure
- **Topics:** hard constraint; soft constraint; threshold; context-dependent safety
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
A constraint may keep both its cost function and threshold fixed, change only part of that specification, or adapt both as the environment evolves. These choices imply different guarantees and evaluation criteria. Any safe baseline in the thesis should declare its constraint function, threshold, and whether either is allowed to change.

## Evidence E6 — Many safe non-stationary methods rely on smooth-change assumptions or provide soft guarantees
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 8, limitations and open problems
- **Claim:** Existing methods often rely on bounded/smooth non-stationarity assumptions and frequently provide probabilistic, expected, or budget-style safety guarantees rather than uninterrupted hard guarantees.
- **Thesis use:** guarantee labeling
- **Topics:** smoothness; probabilistic guarantee; violation budget
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Safety claims in non-stationary RL depend strongly on assumptions about how quickly the environment can change and what prior safety information is available. A guarantee expressed as an expected cost bound, probability bound, or variation budget should not be described as an unconditional hard safety guarantee.

## Avoid overclaiming
This source is a survey and should be used to classify safety/adaptation assumptions and reporting requirements. It does not establish a universal safe continual-RL algorithm or require adaptive constraints in the thesis core.

---

## `SRC-0882A9B2B0` — Assessing Generalization in Deep Reinforcement Learning

- **Συγγραφείς:** Charles Packer; Katelyn Gao; Jernej Kos; Philipp Krähenbühl; Vladlen Koltun; Dawn Song
- **Έτος:** 2018
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** μη στασιμότητα; GridWorld; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-0882A9B2B0.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-0882A9B2B0.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.12282)

## E1 — Interpolation and extrapolation are distinct evaluation regimes

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1
- **Claim:** Generalization to environments similar to training conditions and generalization outside the training distribution are distinct capabilities and should be evaluated separately.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Packer et al. distinguish interpolation, where test-environment parameters remain similar to values encountered during training, from extrapolation, where evaluation occurs in parameter regions outside the training distribution. They report that extrapolation is the more difficult regime and emphasize its relevance to previously unseen deployment conditions.

### Context and limits

This is a static train/test generalization framework. It does not by itself measure online post-change adaptation or recovery.

### Thesis use

Predefine in-range and out-of-range severity bands for environment perturbations rather than collapsing them into one generic robustness score.

### Citation

Packer et al. (2018), Abstract and Section 1.

## E2 — Parameterized environment distributions support reproducible train/test splits

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Environment construction and Figure 1
- **Claim:** Controlled parameter distributions can define nominal, randomized, and non-overlapping extreme test regimes reproducibly.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The benchmark varies selected environment parameters to construct deterministic, randomized, and extreme variants. Random environments sample around default parameter values, while extreme environments sample from ranges that do not overlap the randomized training region. This makes the distinction between nominal conditions, interpolation, and extrapolation explicit in the benchmark design.

### Context and limits

What counts as an extreme region is researcher-defined. The ranges should be fixed before final evaluation and should not be selected after inspecting algorithm results.

### Thesis use

Use seeded configuration generators for train, validation, and held-out test perturbation sets.

### Citation

Packer et al. (2018), environment-design section and Figure 1.

## E3 — Fair comparisons require matched training and tuning protocols

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Experimental methodology
- **Claim:** Algorithm comparisons are more credible when environment exposure, training resources, architectures where possible, and hyperparameter-search procedures are controlled.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The study evaluates vanilla and generalization-oriented methods under a common protocol and explicitly controls training resources and tuning methodology across the compared systems. Its motivation is to reduce the risk that an apparent algorithm advantage is caused by a more favorable implementation or evaluation budget rather than the method itself.

### Context and limits

Equal numbers of trials do not necessarily imply equal compute cost. Environment interactions, wall-clock time, and tuning effort should still be reported separately.

### Thesis use

Keep tuning seeds separate from final evaluation seeds and predefine comparable tuning budgets for each agent family.

### Citation

Packer et al. (2018), experimental methodology.

## E4 — Specialized robust or adaptive methods are not automatically superior to vanilla baselines

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Results and discussion
- **Claim:** In the reported benchmark, vanilla A2C and PPO often generalized better than the specialized EPOpt and RL2 variants, with gains from specialized methods depending strongly on environment and implementation choices.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The controlled evaluation finds that standard A2C and PPO trained across environment variations often outperform the tested EPOpt and RL2 variants. EPOpt improves generalization only in some configurations, while RL2 is difficult to train under the same resource budget. The result argues against assuming that a method designed explicitly for generalization must dominate a simpler baseline.

### Context and limits

These are results for specific algorithms, implementations, architectures, and environments from the study. They do not establish a universal ranking for all robust or meta-RL methods.

### Thesis use

Retain simple baselines before adding more complex adaptation mechanisms and interpret complexity as justified only by measured benefit.

### Citation

Packer et al. (2018), results and discussion.

## E5 — Nominal, interpolation, and extrapolation performance should remain visible separately

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Evaluation metrics
- **Claim:** The benchmark reports nominal, interpolation, and extrapolation behavior as distinct quantities rather than treating all generalization failures as one undifferentiated robustness outcome.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The evaluation defines separate performance summaries for default conditions, randomized in-distribution conditions, and extrapolation conditions. Although the paper aggregates some extrapolation scenarios, the protocol preserves the conceptual distinction between solving the nominal task and retaining performance under increasingly unfamiliar environment parameters.

### Context and limits

Any aggregate score can hide severe failure in one scenario. Per-scenario results should accompany aggregate summaries.

### Thesis use

Report nominal return, in-range robustness, and out-of-range generalization separately, then add online recovery metrics only for experiments that actually adapt after a change.

### Citation

Packer et al. (2018), evaluation-metrics section.

---

## `SRC-09DD20BA85` — Bounded Robustness in Reinforcement Learning via Lexicographic Objectives

- **Συγγραφείς:** Daniel Jarne Ornia; Licio Romao; Lewis Hammond; Manuel Mazo; Alessandro Abate
- **Έτος:** 2022
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** εύρωστη ενισχυτική μάθηση; αβεβαιότητα παρατηρήσεων; μερική παρατηρησιμότητα; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; αβεβαιότητα μεταβάσεων
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-09DD20BA85.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-09DD20BA85.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.15320)

## E1 — Observation corruption induces a disturbed effective policy

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2, Definitions 1–2
- **Claim:** Observational disturbance should be modeled explicitly through an observation-noise kernel, because the agent acts on a corrupted measurement rather than on the true state.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper models an observationally disturbed MDP with a stochastic kernel `T(y|x)` that maps the true state to a possibly corrupted measurement. Since the policy chooses actions from that measurement, the deployed behavior is equivalent to a disturbed effective policy obtained by averaging the original policy through the noise kernel. Robustness regret is then defined as the utility difference between the nominal policy and this disturbed policy.

### Context and limits

This formulation specifically concerns state-observation corruption. Reward noise, delayed observations, or missing observations may require different operators or a different POMDP formulation.

### Thesis use

For observation-noise experiments, store the true state, the observed state, and the disturbance parameters separately, and evaluate clean and disturbed execution explicitly.

### Citation

Jarne Ornia et al. (2024), Section 2, Definitions 1–2.

## E2 — Robustness should be constrained by a nominal-utility tolerance

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Problem 1; lexicographic formulation; LRPG contribution
- **Claim:** The method optimizes robustness only among policies whose nominal utility remains within a specified tolerance of the primary objective.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper formulates the design problem as finding the most robust policy inside an `epsilon`-optimal set for the nominal return. The higher-priority objective is therefore nominal utility, and robustness is optimized subject to an explicit bound on how far the resulting policy may move from the nominal optimum. LRPG implements this lexicographic ordering while retaining the formal convergence structure of the underlying policy-gradient method under the paper's assumptions.

### Context and limits

The tolerance is a design choice, not a value supplied automatically by the theory. The formal guarantees also depend on the policy and convergence assumptions made in the analysis.

### Thesis use

Report nominal return and disturbed return together; do not label a method superior merely because it is robust if that robustness is purchased by a large clean-condition loss.

### Citation

Jarne Ornia et al. (2024), Problem 1 and the LRPG formulation.

## E3 — Robustness experiments should expose several disturbance regimes

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Experimental section; MiniGrid evaluation; discussion
- **Claim:** The empirical study evaluates clean and multiple disturbed conditions rather than treating robustness as a single scalar property.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The authors apply LRPG on top of PPO and A2C in MiniGrid tasks and evaluate policies under clean execution as well as several bounded stochastic and adversarial observation perturbations. The relative behavior of the methods varies across tasks, base algorithms, and robustness objectives, and the discussion notes that explicit model-based filtering may be preferable when a suitable disturbance model is available.

### Context and limits

The paper's representative-agent reporting does not fully characterize training uncertainty. Its quantitative algorithm ranking should not be transferred directly to a new GridWorld benchmark.

### Thesis use

Use multiple observation-noise regimes, but report all evaluation seeds with uncertainty intervals and retain per-scenario results rather than only a representative score.

### Citation

Jarne Ornia et al. (2024), experimental section and discussion.

## E4 — Robustness regret is not a recovery metric

- **Type:** scope inference grounded in the formulation
- **Location:** Sections 2–4
- **Claim:** The paper's robustness objective measures degradation under observational disturbance; it does not measure post-change relearning or time to recovery.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The method compares nominal policy utility with the utility of the noise-altered effective policy and searches for policies that limit this loss. It does not define a changepoint detector, online reset mechanism, context recall, or recovery trajectory after an environmental regime change.

### Thesis use

Keep observational robustness metrics separate from resilience metrics such as degradation depth, recovery time, and post-change adaptation success.

### Citation

Jarne Ornia et al. (2024), method scope, Sections 2–4.

## `SRC-0A4AFAC8E9` — Deep Reinforcement Learning at the Edge of the Statistical Precipice

- **Συγγραφείς:** Rishabh Agarwal; Max Schwarzer; Pablo Samuel Castro; Aaron Courville; Marc G. Bellemare
- **Έτος:** 2021
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; στατιστική αξιολόγηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-0A4AFAC8E9.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-0A4AFAC8E9.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.13264)

## Evidence E1 — Few-run RL comparisons need uncertainty, not only point estimates
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1; Table 1
- **Claim:** Mean or median point estimates from a small number of stochastic training runs can give unreliable impressions of algorithm performance.
- **Thesis use:** statistical protocol
- **Topics:** few-run evaluation; uncertainty; reproducibility
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
With only a handful of runs per task and substantial run-to-run variability, aggregate RL scores are themselves uncertain quantities. Reporting only a mean or median can materially overstate or understate expected performance and can change the apparent ranking of algorithms under new independent runs.

### Thesis-safe implication
No superiority claim in the thesis should rest on one seed or on a point estimate without an uncertainty estimate and the underlying run distribution.

## Evidence E2 — Stratified bootstrap intervals preserve benchmark structure
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4.1 and Table 1
- **Claim:** The paper recommends interval estimates based on stratified bootstrap resampling for aggregate performance in multi-task RL benchmarks.
- **Thesis use:** confidence intervals
- **Topics:** bootstrap; interval estimate; task/run structure
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The proposed bootstrap procedure resamples in a way that respects the organization of scores by task and run. It produces an interval of plausible aggregate-performance values rather than treating the observed aggregate as exact. When comparing two agents, uncertainty in their difference should be estimated directly instead of reasoning mechanically from overlap between two separate intervals.

### Limitation
Bootstrap intervals do not repair biased experiment selection, unequal tuning budgets, or dependence created by an invalid sampling design.

## Evidence E3 — IQM is a useful robust aggregate but should not hide tail failures
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Table 1; Section 4.3
- **Claim:** Interquartile mean is proposed as a robust and statistically efficient aggregate in the few-run regime.
- **Thesis use:** secondary aggregate metric
- **Topics:** IQM; outliers; aggregate performance
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
IQM averages the middle half of the pooled normalized scores, making it less dominated by extreme tasks than the ordinary mean and often less statistically variable than the median. The authors recommend it as part of a broader reporting set rather than as a complete description of performance.

### Thesis-safe implication
Because resilience research cares about rare catastrophic failures, IQM should be paired with failure rates, tail/worst-case summaries, and per-scenario results.

## Evidence E4 — Evaluation protocol differences can exceed algorithm differences
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3 and benchmark case study
- **Claim:** Non-standard choices such as checkpoint/evaluation rules can materially alter reported performance and apparent rankings.
- **Thesis use:** benchmark fairness
- **Topics:** checkpoint selection; evaluation protocol; bias
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Comparisons become unreliable when one method is scored using a different evaluation rule from another, such as selecting a maximum training score instead of using a common final-evaluation protocol. All agents should use the same evaluation budget, checkpoint-selection rule, normalization, and aggregation procedure, with any model selection performed only on validation data.

## Evidence E5 — A fixed seed is not equivalent to statistical reliability
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2 and later reproducibility discussion
- **Claim:** Independent runs can differ because of task stochasticity, exploration, initialization, and software or hardware nondeterminism; fixing a seed does not answer whether conclusions generalize to new random conditions.
- **Thesis use:** seed protocol
- **Topics:** independent runs; random conditions; reproducibility
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Recording seeds is important for reproducibility, but a result obtained under one fixed random condition does not establish expected performance over future random conditions. Evaluation should therefore use multiple independent runs and report their uncertainty.

## Evidence E6 — Effect size and uncertainty are preferable to dichotomous significance claims
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2, confidence-interval discussion
- **Claim:** The authors emphasize interval estimates and effect sizes and caution against binary interpretations based only on statistical-significance thresholds.
- **Thesis use:** result interpretation
- **Topics:** effect size; confidence interval; practical significance
- **Status:** verified

## Avoid overclaiming
This source provides statistical evaluation methodology, not a resilience algorithm. Its benchmark-level aggregate recommendations should complement, not replace, per-shift recovery, safety, and failure analysis.

## `SRC-0A594EACC0` — Cooperative Resilience in Artificial Intelligence Multiagent Systems

- **Συγγραφείς:** Manuela Chacon-Chamorro; Luis Felipe Giraldo; Nicanor Quijano; Vicente Vargas-Panesso; César González; Juan Sebastián Pinzón; Rubén Manrique; Manuel Ríos; Yesid Fonseca; Daniel Gómez-Barrera; Mónica Perdomo-Pérez
- **Έτος:** 2025
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** ανθεκτικότητα και ανάκαμψη; μη στασιμότητα
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-0A594EACC0.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-0A594EACC0.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://doi.org/10.1109/tai.2025.3567371)

## E1 — Resilience spans resistance, recovery, and transformation across a disruptive event

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2, Definition 1
- **Claim:** The paper defines resilience as a temporal capability that includes preparation, resistance, recovery, and transformation in response to a specified disruption.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Chacon-Chamorro et al. define cooperative resilience as the ability of a system involving collective action to anticipate and prepare for disruption, resist its effects, recover afterward, and transform in response to events that threaten joint welfare. The definition therefore treats resilience as a process spanning phases before, during, and after disruption, and it allows past disruptions to influence how the system handles later ones.

### Context and limits

The definition is written for cooperative multi-agent systems. A single-agent thesis can borrow the temporal structure only after replacing joint welfare with explicitly defined task and safety variables.

### Thesis use

Use resilience as a broader temporal concept than zero-shot robustness or adaptation alone. Preserve separate measurements for immediate degradation, recovery, and longer-term improvement.

### Citation

Chacon-Chamorro et al. (2024), Section 2, Definition 1.

## E2 — Reference and performance curves support event-window resilience measurement

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 3.1–3.2
- **Claim:** The proposed measurement method compares time-dependent performance under disruption against a reference trajectory without the disruption.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The methodology first defines time-dependent variables of interest and records both a reference curve under normal conditions and a performance curve under disruptive conditions. Each disruptive event is then analyzed inside a time window containing the event and its subsequent failure and recovery behavior. The resilience calculation compares the performance and reference trajectories so that both the magnitude and temporal duration of degradation and recovery contribute to the result.

### Context and limits

The reference trajectory is not required to be ideal; it represents expected behavior without the disruptive event. Window placement and variable orientation must be defined consistently.

### Thesis use

Use matched-seed nominal and perturbed runs to construct normalized performance-deficit, area-loss, and recovery-time metrics while retaining the raw curves.

### Citation

Chacon-Chamorro et al. (2024), Sections 3.1–3.2.

## E3 — Repeated disruptions should be evaluated as a sequence, not only averaged independently

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3, Stages III–IV
- **Claim:** The method explicitly aggregates event-level resilience over successive disruptions and rewards improvement while penalizing deterioration.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

After computing resilience for individual disruptive events, the methodology combines those event-level measurements across time. The aggregation is designed to distinguish systems whose response improves after previous disruptions from systems whose resilience degrades over repeated events. A final stage also combines multiple welfare variables, so poor behavior on one important dimension can remain visible.

### Context and limits

The paper's exact nonlinear aggregation is one design choice and may be sensitive to event ordering and metric scaling.

### Thesis use

Report each occurrence separately and include a metric for change in recovery quality across repeated visits to the same or related regime, rather than relying only on a grand mean.

### Citation

Chacon-Chamorro et al. (2024), Section 3, Stages III–IV.

## E4 — Multiple variables can reveal delayed or indirect consequences of disruption

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Experimental results, Common Harvest Open case study
- **Claim:** Different performance variables can exhibit different temporal responses to the same disruption, so a single task score can miss delayed or indirect damage.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

In the case study, environmental resource disruption affects indicators such as resource availability, sustainability, inequality, and collective hunger on different timescales. The resulting reference and performance curves show that one disruptive event can have an immediate effect on one indicator and delayed consequences on others.

### Context and limits

The particular welfare variables are domain-specific and come from a cooperative social-dilemma setting.

### Thesis use

Keep task return, success rate, safety violations, intervention cost, and recovery quality as separate reported quantities before any composite resilience score is constructed.

### Citation

Chacon-Chamorro et al. (2024), experimental case study and resilience analysis.

## E5 — The source is methodological support, not a direct single-agent algorithm baseline

- **Type:** scope inference grounded in the paper
- **Location:** Abstract; Sections 1–5
- **Claim:** The contribution is a definition and measurement methodology for cooperative resilience, validated in a multi-agent environment; it does not provide a directly comparable single-agent adaptation algorithm for the thesis benchmark.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper's central contribution is conceptual and metric-oriented. Its experiments use cooperative systems with RL- and LLM-based agents in Melting Pot 2.0, so its value for a single-agent GridWorld thesis lies primarily in the temporal decomposition and measurement principles rather than in algorithm ranking.

### Thesis use

Use the source for resilience definitions and repeated-event metric design, not as evidence that a particular multi-agent architecture should be implemented.

### Citation

Chacon-Chamorro et al. (2024), overall scope.

## `SRC-0A8E4489E8` — https---proceedings.mlr.press-v134-wei21b-wei21b.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2021
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** μη στασιμότητα
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-0A8E4489E8.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-0A8E4489E8.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v134/wei21b/wei21b.pdf)

## E1 — Knowing the number or total amount of change is an oracle advantage

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1
- **Claim:** Many non-stationary learning algorithms require prior knowledge of how many changes will occur or how much the environment will vary, while MASTER is designed to adapt without receiving those quantities as inputs.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Wei and Luo identify prior knowledge of the degree of non-stationarity as a central limitation of earlier methods. They use `L` for the number of changes and `Delta` for the amount of change and design a black-box reduction whose dynamic-regret guarantees adapt to these quantities without requiring the learner to know them in advance.

### Context and limits

A hyperparameter chosen from held-out development data is not the same as knowing the future test changepoints, but any use of true `L`, `Delta`, or change times should be labeled oracle information.

### Thesis use

Separate deployable non-oracle agents from periodic-reset or variation-budget baselines that use privileged knowledge.

### Citation

Wei and Luo (2021), Abstract and Section 1.

## E2 — Fixed restart periods, windows, and discount factors encode a change timescale

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 1 and Table 1 discussion
- **Claim:** A fixed forgetting schedule can become suboptimal when its timescale does not match the unknown rate of environmental change.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper contrasts MASTER with approaches based on restarting at a predetermined period, retaining a fixed-size window, or discounting old data with a fixed factor. In its regret analysis, these fixed-timescale strategies can have worse dependence on the actual number of changes when that scale is not known beforehand.

### Context and limits

This is a theoretical regret comparison. A fixed recency baseline can still be strong in a controlled environment whose shift frequency happens to match its tuning.

### Thesis use

For every forgetting baseline, record how the reset period, window length, or decay factor was selected and prohibit tuning on final test shifts.

### Citation

Wei and Luo (2021), Section 1 and Table 1 discussion.

## E3 — Multiple learner timescales can expose changes that a long-lived instance misses

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2.3; Figure 1
- **Claim:** MASTER schedules base-algorithm instances with different durations so recently started instances remain sensitive to changes at different scales.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The reduction runs base learners over a hierarchy of randomized time intervals. Shorter-lived instances can adapt quickly to a newly changed environment, and their behavior can reveal that the performance expectations of a longer-running instance are no longer consistent with current observations. The design therefore covers several possible change scales without committing to one fixed window in advance.

### Context and limits

The full construction relies on confidence and regret properties of the base algorithm. A simple comparison of two moving averages is only an approximation to this idea and does not inherit MASTER's theorem.

### Thesis use

A lightweight short-versus-long evidence score may be explored as a pilot detector, but it must be evaluated as its own heuristic.

### Citation

Wei and Luo (2021), Section 2.3 and Figure 1.

## E4 — Detection and restart are separate stages in the adaptive mechanism

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Algorithm 3; Section 3.2
- **Claim:** MASTER applies stationarity tests and restarts its scheduling block when evidence indicates the current near-stationary assumptions have failed.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The algorithm maintains tests based on the performance and optimistic estimates of scheduled base instances. When a stationarity test is violated, the current block terminates and a new block begins. The evidence used to infer non-stationarity and the restart action taken afterward are therefore distinct parts of the procedure.

### Context and limits

The paper's thresholds are derived from assumptions about the base learner and cannot be transferred numerically to an unrelated Q-learning implementation.

### Thesis use

Log detector events and reset events separately, including false resets and any delay between a true change, detection, and policy intervention.

### Citation

Wei and Luo (2021), Algorithm 3 and Section 3.2.

## E5 — Adaptation to unknown `L` and `Delta` depends on base-algorithm guarantees

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Theorem 2 and Assumption 1
- **Claim:** MASTER's parameter-free dynamic-regret guarantee is conditional on the plugged-in stationary learner satisfying specified optimism and regret properties.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The black-box result does not turn an arbitrary learning rule into an optimally adaptive non-stationary algorithm. Its theorem assumes a base algorithm with a particular near-stationary performance guarantee and confidence structure; under those conditions, the wrapper adapts its regret to the realized number and amount of changes without knowing them as inputs.

### Context and limits

Vanilla tabular Q-learning does not automatically inherit this result merely by adding a reset trigger.

### Thesis use

Use MASTER as theoretical support for non-oracle multi-timescale adaptation, not as a theorem about the thesis's simpler baselines.

### Citation

Wei and Luo (2021), Assumption 1 and Theorem 2.

## E6 — Fully prior-free infinite-horizon adaptation still has extra cost and open cases

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 4.2 and 5
- **Claim:** In communicating infinite-horizon MDPs, removing knowledge of both non-stationarity quantities and structural parameters such as the maximum diameter introduces additional overhead, and the fully optimal case remains unresolved in the paper.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The strongest infinite-horizon results require either information about the non-stationarity or the maximum MDP diameter. When none of these quantities is known, the paper combines MASTER with an additional bandit-over-RL layer and pays an extra regret term; eliminating this cost is left open.

### Context and limits

The phrase “without prior knowledge” in the title concerns the degree of non-stationarity under the stated assumptions, not the absence of every possible structural assumption or tuning parameter.

### Thesis use

Document every source of prior knowledge and structural information available to each agent rather than using a binary “parameter-free” label.

### Citation

Wei and Luo (2021), Sections 4.2 and 5.

## `SRC-0AEF7EF16A` — A Bayesian Approach to Robust Reinforcement Learning - Association for Uncertainty in Artificial Int

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** —
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** εύρωστη ενισχυτική μάθηση; ασφαλής ενισχυτική μάθηση; αβεβαιότητα παρατηρήσεων; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; στατιστική αξιολόγηση
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-0AEF7EF16A.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-0AEF7EF16A.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://auai.org/uai2019/proceedings/papers/228.pdf)

## Evidence E1 — Fixed worst-case uncertainty sets can be overly conservative
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1
- **Claim:** Robust MDP policies can sacrifice substantial nominal utility when tractability assumptions or oversized uncertainty sets make the worst-case model too pessimistic.
- **Thesis use:** robust-policy trade-off
- **Topics:** conservativeness; uncertainty set; nominal utility
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The paper argues that robust planning can become unnecessarily pessimistic because rectangular uncertainty sets allow independent worst-case transitions at each state-action pair and because uncertainty sets are difficult to specify tightly. A policy can therefore protect against combinations of adverse transitions that are unlikely to occur together and lose useful nominal performance.

### Thesis-safe implication
A robustness comparison should report clean/nominal utility together with disturbed performance; a small degradation is not automatically desirable if the pre-change policy already performs poorly.

## Evidence E2 — Posterior uncertainty can guide robust exploration
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 4–6; Algorithm 1
- **Claim:** URBE uses posterior uncertainty over robust Q-values to guide exploration while updating transition uncertainty from observed experience.
- **Thesis use:** uncertainty-aware adaptation background
- **Topics:** Bayesian uncertainty; URBE; exploration
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The method places Dirichlet priors over transition probabilities, updates the posterior as new transitions are observed, constructs posterior uncertainty sets around the current transition estimate, and derives an upper bound on the posterior variance of robust Q-values. The deep implementation uses this uncertainty as an exploration bonus so that uncertain state-action regions receive more attention while the agent preserves a robust value criterion.

### Limitation
The uncertainty bonus is not an explicit calibrated changepoint detector. It is an exploration/adaptation signal whose statistical meaning depends on the Bayesian model and approximations.

## Evidence E3 — Safe inactivity is not the same as useful robustness
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** GridWorld/Mars Rover experiments
- **Claim:** A fixed robust policy can avoid catastrophic states yet fail to accomplish the nominal task, while uncertainty-aware robust exploration can improve the robustness–performance trade-off.
- **Thesis use:** success/safety metric separation
- **Topics:** GridWorld; catastrophic failure; task completion
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
In the Mars Rover experiment, a fixed robust DQN avoids failure but can become so conservative that it does not reach the goal even under nominal conditions. The uncertainty-aware method is designed to retain robustness to misspecified transitions while still exploring enough to make progress. Avoiding failure alone is therefore not sufficient evidence of a practically useful resilient policy.

## Evidence E4 — Online changing-dynamics experiments expose recovery trajectories
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 7.3; changing CartPole experiment
- **Claim:** After an abrupt dynamics change, DQN-URBE is reported to regain high reward faster than a fixed robust DQN in the studied setting.
- **Thesis use:** recovery-curve motivation
- **Topics:** changing dynamics; recovery speed; continued learning
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
After initial convergence, the experiment changes the CartPole pole length and continues training. The uncertainty-aware robust method initially learns more slowly but, after the dynamics change, returns to high reward substantially faster in the reported curves than the robust method using a fixed uncertainty set.

### Limitation
The paper does not define a universal recovery threshold or provide a modern per-seed confidence interval for recovery time. The thesis should predefine a threshold and compute recovery metrics per run.

## Evidence E5 — Deep URBE is an approximation to the theoretical construction
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 4–6 and discussion of the scalable deep algorithm
- **Claim:** The theoretical URBE derivation uses assumptions that are relaxed or approximated in the neural implementation.
- **Thesis use:** theory/practice boundary
- **Topics:** assumptions; deep approximation; uncertainty
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The theoretical variance recursion is derived under structural assumptions such as a fixed policy and an acyclic worst-case transition graph, whereas DQN-URBE uses learned neural approximations and a changing policy. The formal bound should therefore not be presented as a direct guarantee for every behavior of the deep implementation.

## Avoid overclaiming
This source demonstrates one Bayesian robust-adaptation approach and supports reporting uncertainty and recovery trajectories. It does not imply that posterior variance is a calibrated environment-change detector or that the deep method is required for a lightweight tabular benchmark.

## `SRC-0F8A6588DC` — NovGrid: A Flexible Grid World for Evaluating Agent Response to Novelty

- **Συγγραφείς:** Jonathan Balloch; Zhiyu Lin; Mustafa Hussain; Aarun Srinivas; Robert Wright; Xiangyu Peng; Julia Kim; Mark Riedl
- **Έτος:** 2022
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** ανθεκτικότητα και ανάκαμψη; μη στασιμότητα; GridWorld; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks; πράκτορες τεχνητής νοημοσύνης
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-0F8A6588DC.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-0F8A6588DC.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.12117)

## E1 — Novelty is a sudden change to environment mechanics or properties
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction; Novelty Background and Related Work
- **Claim:** NovGrid defines novelty as an unexpected change to the mechanics or properties of the environment rather than an isolated outlier under an otherwise fixed distribution.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Balloch et al. study novelty as a temporal change that transforms the sequential decision problem after an agent has learned under a previous regime. They explicitly distinguish this setting from ordinary outlier detection, because the post-novelty observations belong to a changed environment rather than being aberrant samples that should simply be rejected.

### Context and limits
The paper focuses on abrupt injected changes. Slow continuous drift and independent observation noise require separate formulations.

### Thesis use
Reserve `novelty` or `sudden_shift` for temporal environment changes and do not treat isolated prediction-error spikes as proof that the environment has changed.

### Citation
Balloch et al., Abstract and Novelty Background and Related Work.

## E2 — The ontology separates object/action changes and their effect on the solution set
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** An Ontology of Novelty for Sequential Decision Making; Table 1
- **Claim:** The proposed ontology distinguishes object versus action novelties, unary versus relational changes, and barrier, delta, or shortcut effects on optimal solutions.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The ontology classifies whether a novelty changes objects or action mechanics, whether the changed property is unary or relational, and whether the transformation makes optimal solutions longer, leaves their length broadly unchanged, or creates shorter solutions. The taxonomy is then instantiated with concrete MiniGrid examples such as key–door changes, transition determinism changes, and action-effect changes.

### Context and limits
The ontology is designed for sequential decision making and is not an exhaustive taxonomy of every uncertainty or distribution-shift mechanism.

### Thesis use
Construct a factorized perturbation matrix so structural changes are not mixed arbitrarily with reward drift, observation corruption, or bounded stochastic model error.

### Citation
Balloch et al., An Ontology of Novelty for Sequential Decision Making, Table 1.

## E3 — The interface can remain fixed while the underlying MDP changes
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Ontology assumptions; Novelty MiniGrid
- **Claim:** NovGrid keeps the dimensions of the action and observation interfaces compatible across the change while allowing action effects, object properties, reachable states, and dynamics to differ.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The framework assumes that the number of actions and the size and shape of observations remain consistent before and after novelty injection. Nevertheless, actions may acquire different effects and observations or states that never occurred before the change may become reachable afterward. The wrapper injects the novelty by switching the environment's reset and grid-generation behavior at a specified episode.

### Context and limits
A fixed interface does not imply a stationary MDP or equal task difficulty across regimes.

### Thesis use
Define interface invariants separately from transition/reward invariants and add validation tests that confirm only the intended mechanism changes.

### Citation
Balloch et al., ontology assumptions and Novelty MiniGrid section.

## E4 — Adaptation should be decomposed into immediate robustness, final performance, and adaptation efficiency
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Evaluation and Baseline; Figure 2
- **Claim:** NovGrid proposes multiple metrics because different parts of the post-change learning curve capture different capabilities.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The framework includes metrics for the performance of the pre-novelty policy immediately after the change, the final post-novelty performance reached after additional learning, the amount of interaction required to converge, and one-shot performance after very little post-change experience. These quantities intentionally separate immediate resistance from later adaptation.

### Context and limits
The paper's specific metric named “resilience” is one operational definition and should not be treated as a universal definition of resilience.

### Thesis use
Report immediate degradation, adaptation success, recovery time or interactions, recovered performance, and one-shot response as separate metrics before considering any aggregate score.

### Citation
Balloch et al., Evaluation and Baseline, Figure 2.

## E5 — Continued learning is a necessary baseline but not a dedicated novelty-adaptation mechanism
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Evaluation and Baseline; Figure 3
- **Claim:** A standard RL agent that simply continues training after novelty provides a useful reference for determining whether a specialized adaptation mechanism adds value.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The baseline PPO agent in the NovGrid experiment has no novelty-specific adaptation component. After the injected change, it continues reinforcement learning from task reward. Its post-change learning curve therefore shows what can be achieved by ordinary continued learning under the same changed environment without an explicit novelty detector or specialized response mechanism.

### Context and limits
Calling this baseline a lower bound in the paper does not prove that it will be worse than every specialized method in every environment.

### Thesis use
Retain a `continue_learning` baseline alongside frozen-policy, full-reset, and detector-triggered variants.

### Citation
Balloch et al., Evaluation and Baseline, Figure 3.

## E6 — Reward changes are outside the specific NovGrid ontology used in this paper
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** ontology assumptions
- **Claim:** The paper keeps the agent mission and extrinsic reward structure fixed across novelty and focuses on changes to environment properties and dynamics.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The authors assume that the agent's mission remains the same before and after novelty injection and state that changes to the goal or extrinsic reward structure are left to future integration with continual- and multitask-learning settings.

### Thesis use
Treat reward drift as a separate experimental axis rather than citing NovGrid as direct evidence for reward-change adaptation.

### Citation
Balloch et al., ontology assumptions.

## `SRC-13B66B20D8` — BALLOCH-DISSERTATION-2024.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2024
- **Τύπος:** διπλωματική ή διατριβή
- **Θέματα / tags:** GridWorld; ενισχυτική μάθηση με μοντέλο; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-13B66B20D8.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-13B66B20D8.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** —

## E1 — Source-task learning and post-novelty adaptation are separate phases

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Chapters 3–4; Figure 4.2
- **Claim:** The evaluation distinguishes learning a stationary source task from adapting after a novelty changes the target environment.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Balloch's experimental framework first allows an agent to learn the original task and then introduces a novelty that changes the environment. Performance commonly drops when the new task begins, after which the experiment measures how effectively the agent learns the altered reward or transition structure.

### Thesis use

Use explicit pre-change and post-change phases and preserve the full recovery curve rather than reporting only final return.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Chapters 3–4 and Figure 4.2.

## E2 — Adaptive efficiency measures successful post-change convergence time

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Chapter 4; Table 4.2
- **Claim:** Adaptive efficiency is the number of interaction steps from the beginning of the novel task until convergence on the second task, computed only for runs that converge on both tasks.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Lower adaptive-efficiency values indicate faster successful adaptation, but the metric conditions on convergence. Runs that fail to learn either task do not enter the reported mean and variance.

### Context and limits

Ignoring failed runs can create survivorship bias and make a method look fast precisely because unsuccessful seeds have been excluded.

### Thesis use

Always report adaptation/recovery time together with the fraction of seeds that reach the corresponding threshold.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Chapter 4 and Table 4.2.

## E3 — Adaptation speed and final post-change performance are distinct

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Chapter 4; Figure 4.2 and surrounding metric discussion
- **Claim:** An agent can adapt quickly without recovering to its previous performance level, so time-to-convergence and final target-task performance measure different outcomes.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The dissertation evaluates how quickly the learning curve stabilizes after novelty and separately how well the agent ultimately performs on the new task. A short adaptation interval does not imply that the new steady-state return matches the source-task performance.

### Thesis use

Report both time-to-recovery/threshold and final post-change return.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Chapter 4 and Figure 4.2.

## E4 — Transfer area under the curve summarizes the adaptation trajectory but is conditional

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Chapter 4; Table 4.3
- **Claim:** Transfer AUC combines source-task final reward with the area under the target-task reward curve and is reported only for runs that converge on the source task.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The transfer-AUC metric rewards both competence before novelty and strong reward accumulation while learning the new task. Its reported statistics exclude runs that failed to converge on the first task, so the conditioning rule is part of the metric definition.

### Thesis use

If an area-under-recovery score is used, publish the exact normalization and treatment of failed runs.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Table 4.3.

## E5 — Exploration effectiveness depends on the novelty and environment

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Chapter 4; Figures 4.3–4.5
- **Claim:** The dissertation does not find one exploration method that dominates across all tested novelty conditions.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The exploration experiments compare several strategies across discrete and continuous-control novelty tasks. Relative performance changes with the novelty and environment, illustrating that an exploration mechanism that accelerates adaptation in one setting can be less effective in another.

### Thesis use

Evaluate exploration and recency mechanisms across more than one shift type before drawing comparative conclusions.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Chapter 4 and Figures 4.3–4.5.

## E6 — NovGrid supports hidden semantic or rule changes

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Chapter 3; Figure 3.1
- **Claim:** The benchmark includes novelties that alter task semantics without directly revealing the new rule to the agent.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Examples include changing which key opens a door and changing lava from a terminal hazard into a traversable or safe region. The visual objects can remain recognizable while the consequences of interacting with them change, requiring the agent to discover the new semantics through experience.

### Thesis use

Include rule/semantic shifts that do not provide an oracle change label.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Chapter 3 and Figure 3.1.

## E7 — Different replay objectives can require different priorities

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Chapter 5, Dual Objective Priority Sampling
- **Claim:** In the dissertation's model-based RL setting, world-model learning and actor–critic learning can benefit from different priority signals applied to the same replay transitions.
- **Status:** verified

### Context and limits

This is motivation for component-specific replay design in model-based RL, not evidence that the thesis's tabular agents require DOPS.

### Thesis use

If a model-based ablation is added, document replay/recency policy separately for each learning component.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Chapter 5.

## E8 — Non-convergence is an outcome, not missing data

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Tables 4.2–4.3 and appendix metric notes
- **Claim:** Several adaptation metrics are conditional on convergence, so convergence frequency must be reported as a separate result.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use

For every time-to-threshold or convergence statistic, report the denominator and the percentage of runs that successfully reached the threshold.

### Citation

Balloch (2024), Tables 4.2–4.3 and appendix metric notes.

## `SRC-153C917DE1` — https---proceedings.mlr.press-v199-luketina22a-luketina22a.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2018
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** μη στασιμότητα; GridWorld; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-153C917DE1.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-153C917DE1.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v199/luketina22a/luketina22a.pdf)

## E1 — Context can drive online adaptation of learning hyperparameters

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 1 and 3
- **Claim:** Contextual meta-gradients replace a fixed meta-parameter with a learned function of selected statistics that can signal changes in the task or agent performance.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Luketina et al. let a meta-parameter be predicted from contextual features such as reward statistics, temporal-difference errors, values, or task beliefs. When the learning process has repeatable structure, the learned context-to-hyperparameter function can associate a performance change with a useful response, such as increasing exploration after a task switch.

### Context and limits

The context is not a ground-truth changepoint label. A reward drop may also be caused by stochastic variation, so context-triggered adaptation can overreact if the selected statistics are not specific enough.

### Thesis use

A lightweight adaptive-exploration baseline may change epsilon or another exploration parameter from a documented context signal, but false-trigger behavior must be measured.

### Citation

Luketina et al. (2022), Sections 1 and 3.

## E2 — Small gridworlds can isolate reward-only and reward-plus-transition switches

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4; Appendix A.2
- **Claim:** The paper uses a single stream of interaction in controlled gridworlds where either the reward mapping alone or both rewards and transitions change regularly.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

In Two Colors, a 5×5 gridworld periodically changes which object produces positive reward, creating a clear reward-rule switch. In Switching MDPs, the agent moves through a sequence of gridworlds whose reward and transition functions are randomly generated, so both the objective and environment dynamics can change. The agent experiences these changes in one continuous learning stream rather than restarting training for each task.

### Context and limits

The changes are discrete and regular by design. They do not represent the full range of unpredictable real-world non-stationarity.

### Thesis use

Maintain separate reward-only and combined reward/transition shift scenarios.

### Citation

Luketina et al. (2022), Section 4 and Appendix A.2.

## E3 — Contextual meta-gradients can accelerate post-switch learning in the studied environments

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 6, experiments on Two Colors and Switching MDPs
- **Claim:** Providing informative context makes meta-parameter schedules react more quickly to task switches and improves lifetime performance relative to context-free variants in the reported settings.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

In Two Colors, context-free meta-gradients tend toward nearly constant schedules, whereas reward-context variants learn exploration schedules that change when performance drops after a switch. In Switching MDPs, contextual information also improves performance, including in the large task set where exact tasks are unlikely to repeat.

### Context and limits

The effect depends on the selected context features, meta-parameterization, tuning, and regular task-change process. The paper does not provide a universal post-change recovery-time guarantee.

### Thesis use

If an adaptive exploration mechanism is tested, report explicit recovery metrics instead of relying only on lifetime return.

### Citation

Luketina et al. (2022), Section 6.

## E4 — Non-stationarity has a rate beyond which adaptation cannot keep up

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 6.4 and corresponding appendix experiments
- **Claim:** As task switches become more frequent, the advantage of adaptive meta-gradient methods can shrink or disappear because the agent receives too little stationary interaction to learn effectively.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The experiments vary the interval between environment changes. Rich-context methods remain useful across a broader range of switch frequencies than context-free methods, but sufficiently rapid switching eventually prevents meaningful learning and removes the relative performance advantage.

### Context and limits

The numerical switch intervals are specific to the study. They should not be reused as universal thresholds.

### Thesis use

Treat change frequency as an experimental factor, include an intentionally difficult fast-switch regime, and report unrecovered-event rate.

### Citation

Luketina et al. (2022), Section 6.4 and appendix frequency experiments.

## E5 — Contextual adaptation depends on repeatable or generalizable context structure

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 3–4; conclusion
- **Claim:** Contextual meta-gradients are useful only to the extent that the chosen features let the learned meta-function generalize to recurring or related learning conditions.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The approach assumes that low-dimensional context features contain information relevant to good hyperparameter settings and that the learned function can generalize across those contexts. The paper emphasizes repeated structure as a favorable case while also testing a large set of Switching MDPs where exact tasks rarely recur and context generalization is harder.

### Thesis use

Do not treat a context signal as inherently informative. Validate it on held-out regimes and compare it against a fixed-hyperparameter baseline.

### Citation

Luketina et al. (2022), Sections 3–4 and conclusion.

## `SRC-19858252B7` — Reinforcement Learning by Guided Safe Exploration

- **Συγγραφείς:** Qisong Yang; Thiago D. Simão; Nils Jansen; Simon H. Tindemans; Matthijs T. J. Spaan
- **Έτος:** 2023
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** ασφαλής ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-19858252B7.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-19858252B7.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.14316)

## E1 — Safe transfer should measure safety and task progress separately

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4.2; Figure 2
- **Claim:** The paper distinguishes safety jump-start and time to safety from return jump-start and time to optimal task performance.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Yang et al. define transfer metrics that separate how safely an agent begins the target task from how quickly it becomes safe, and separately measure the initial task-performance advantage and time required to reach optimal reward performance. The framework therefore makes explicit that safe behavior and rapid task learning can improve at different rates.

### Context and limits

The transfer metrics compare a learner that receives source knowledge with a learner trained from scratch. A gain in safety does not imply a gain in task-recovery speed, and vice versa.

### Thesis use

For context recall or pretrained safety mechanisms, report initial safety, time to safety, return jump-start, and time to task recovery separately.

### Citation

Yang et al. (2023), Section 4.2 and Figure 2.

## E2 — The safe guide is a prior capability learned in a controlled source environment

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Sections 1 and 4
- **Claim:** SaGui obtains safety knowledge through prior reward-free constrained training in a controlled source task and transfers that policy to the target learner.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The framework first trains a task-agnostic guide using safety costs in a controlled environment where unsafe interactions are permitted. After the target reward is revealed, the guide is transferred to the target task, used to generate safer initial behavior, and distilled into a student whose reliance on the guide is gradually reduced.

### Context and limits

Improved initial safety is therefore not free: it depends on source-task interactions, a mapping between source and target state spaces, shared actions, and an assumption that relevant safety dynamics transfer.

### Thesis use

Include source/pretraining interactions and prior model/task assumptions in the resource and fairness ledger.

### Citation

Yang et al. (2023), Abstract and Sections 1 and 4.

## E3 — Transfer requires explicit source–target compatibility assumptions

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4.1, Assumption 1 and source–target mapping
- **Claim:** The guide can be reused because source and target tasks share an action space and are connected through a state mapping, while the safety-relevant dynamics are assumed to be sufficiently compatible.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The target policy induced by the guide applies a mapping from target states to source states and reuses the source action distribution. The construction assumes the same action space and relies on the source environment preserving the safety information needed in the target.

### Context and limits

A structural shift that invalidates this mapping or changes safety dynamics can turn prior knowledge into negative transfer.

### Thesis use

Include a source–target mismatch ablation and a no-transfer baseline for any pretrained guide or context-recall mechanism.

### Citation

Yang et al. (2023), Section 4.1 and Assumption 1.

## E4 — Guidance and student learning are distinct components

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 1 and 4; Figure 1
- **Claim:** SaGui composes behavior from a pretrained safety guide and a target-task student, while policy distillation transfers guide behavior into the student over time.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The guide initially contributes strongly to behavior so the target agent can collect safer trajectories. The student simultaneously learns the revealed target objective and is regularized toward guide behavior while it remains unreliable. As training progresses, the framework reduces the guide's influence.

### Context and limits

The guide is not an environmental change detector, and the student's policy improvement should not be credited entirely to the guide.

### Thesis use

Log the fraction of actions attributable to guidance, guide interventions, and student-only performance separately.

### Citation

Yang et al. (2023), Sections 1 and 4 and Figure 1.

---

## `SRC-19C2E91926` — Benchmark for Out-of-Distribution Detection in Deep Reinforcement Learning

- **Συγγραφείς:** Aaqib Parvez Mohammed; Matias Valdenegro-Toro
- **Έτος:** 2021
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; στατιστική αξιολόγηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-19C2E91926.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-19C2E91926.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.02694)

## E1 — RL-specific OOD evaluation needs environment-level shifts
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Sections 1–2
- **Claim:** The paper motivates an OOD benchmark for reinforcement-learning policies because most established OOD evaluations come from static supervised classification rather than agent–environment interaction.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Mohammed and Valdenegro-Toro argue that RL policies can encounter deployment conditions not represented in training, but the available OOD-detection literature and benchmarks are dominated by image-classification settings. Their benchmark therefore constructs altered RL environments so uncertainty methods can be evaluated on observations generated under changed environment conditions.

### Thesis use
Evaluate detectors on controlled environment perturbations rather than only injecting arbitrary anomalies into a static feature dataset.

### Citation
Mohammed and Valdenegro-Toro (2021), Abstract and Sections 1–2.

## E2 — Dynamics changes and observation corruptions are different OOD mechanisms
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4; Table 1
- **Claim:** The benchmark generates OOD conditions either by changing physical environment parameters or by corrupting the observation channel.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
For CartPole and Pendulum, the authors alter physical parameters such as gravity, masses, lengths, force, speed, and torque. For Pong, they instead corrupt visual observations using noise, blur, and related image corruptions. These two procedures modify different parts of the agent–environment system even though both are used to construct OOD test conditions.

### Thesis use
Keep `transition/dynamics_shift` and `observation_corruption` as separate perturbation categories and report their detector results separately.

### Citation
Mohammed and Valdenegro-Toro (2021), Section 4 and Table 1.

## E3 — OOD performance should be measured across perturbation severity
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4; benchmark parameter grids
- **Claim:** The benchmark evaluates multiple magnitudes of physical-parameter change and several severity levels of observation corruption.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Rather than defining one binary altered environment, the benchmark sweeps parameter values away from their defaults and uses several corruption strengths. This allows detector behavior to be examined as the test condition moves farther from training conditions.

### Thesis use
Report detection quality, false alarms, missed changes, and recovery behavior as functions of shift severity rather than only at one hand-picked perturbation level.

### Citation
Mohammed and Valdenegro-Toro (2021), Section 4.

## E4 — Ensembles performed best among the three uncertainty baselines tested here
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; experimental results
- **Claim:** In the reported benchmark, ensembles had stronger OOD-detection performance and lower variability across environments than the tested MC Dropout and MC DropConnect methods.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The study compares Monte Carlo Dropout, Monte Carlo DropConnect, and independently initialized model ensembles as uncertainty estimators. Within these experiments, the ensemble approach provides the strongest overall OOD-detection results and lower standard deviation across several benchmark environments.

### Context and limits
This is a benchmark-specific empirical ordering among three neural uncertainty methods. It does not establish that ensembles are universally optimal, especially for tabular agents or sequential changepoint detection.

### Thesis use
Treat ensembles as optional neural UQ background, not a required core detector.

### Citation
Mohammed and Valdenegro-Toro (2021), Abstract and results.

## E5 — Static OOD discrimination is not an online changepoint detector
- **Type:** scope inference grounded in the benchmark
- **Location:** Experimental protocol
- **Claim:** The benchmark distinguishes in-distribution from OOD conditions but does not evaluate detection delay, false-alarm processes, or missed changepoints in a piecewise-stationary online sequence.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The paper evaluates whether uncertainty methods assign distinguishable scores to data produced by ordinary versus modified environments. It does not define a temporal decision rule that observes a stream, declares a change at an estimated time, and is scored for how early or falsely it triggers.

### Thesis use
A detector inspired by OOD uncertainty must still be calibrated and evaluated with false-alarm rate, detection delay, missed-change rate, and threshold-selection protocol.

### Citation
Mohammed and Valdenegro-Toro (2021), benchmark methodology.

## E6 — OOD detection does not imply adaptation
- **Type:** thesis-scope synthesis
- **Location:** Overall paper
- **Claim:** Detecting that an observation or environment is outside the training distribution does not specify how the policy should adapt afterward.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Keep `detector_score`, `change_decision`, and `adaptation_response` as three separate components in the architecture and evaluation.

### Citation
Mohammed and Valdenegro-Toro (2021), overall scope.
