## `SRC-3F84F52F97` — CARL: A Benchmark for Contextual and Adaptive Reinforcement Learning

- **Συγγραφείς:** Carolin Benjamins; Theresa Eimer; Frederik Schubert; André Biedenkapp; Bodo Rosenhahn; Frank Hutter; Marius Lindauer
- **Έτος:** 2021
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** GridWorld; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-3F84F52F97.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-3F84F52F97.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.02102)

## E1 — Contextual MDPs represent families of related task instances

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2
- **Claim:** CARL models related environment instances with common state and action spaces but context-dependent transition and reward functions.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Benjamins et al. formulate CARL environments as contextual MDPs. Each sampled instance is defined by a context and retains a common task interface, while transition dynamics, rewards, and the subset of reachable states may differ across instances.

### Context and limits

A shared interface does not imply identical difficulty or the same optimal policy.

### Thesis use

Represent GridWorld configurations as context-resolved MDP instances with recorded context values.

### Citation

Benjamins et al. (2021), Section 2.

## E2 — Explicit and implicit context provide different information to the agent

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4
- **Claim:** Explicit context is supplied directly, while implicit context must be inferred from observations, entangling representation learning with policy generalization.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

CARL distinguishes context information that is directly available to the policy from context that is hidden inside the ordinary observation. When context is implicit, the agent must first extract the relevant environmental factors before it can condition its behavior on them, so representation learning becomes part of the generalization problem.

### Thesis use

State explicitly whether the current regime or change variable is observable, hidden, or available only to an oracle baseline.

### Citation

Benjamins et al. (2021), Section 4.

## E3 — Reproducible generalization requires explicit context bounds and distributions

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 1–2 and benchmark design
- **Claim:** CARL provides defined ranges and sampling distributions for context variables so train and test instance populations can be reconstructed.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The benchmark was motivated in part by inconsistent hand-designed task variations across prior studies. CARL instead exposes context features together with bounds and distributions, enabling researchers to specify how training and test instances are sampled and how far a test context lies from the training distribution.

### Context and limits

Reproducibility of a range does not establish that the chosen severity is scientifically appropriate.

### Thesis use

Persist resolved context values, generation distributions, seeds, and severity bands for every run.

### Citation

Benjamins et al. (2021), Sections 1–2.

## E4 — Multiple seeds and sampled contexts are needed for generalization claims

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Experimental methodology
- **Claim:** The CARL demonstrations evaluate agents over multiple random seeds and many sampled contexts rather than relying on one environment instance.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper's main demonstrations use repeated training runs and broad context sampling, with implementation and reproduction details provided alongside the experiments. The purpose is to characterize behavior over a population of environment instances rather than a single favorable configuration.

### Thesis use

Use multiple agent seeds and multiple environment instances within each severity condition; determine the final counts from pilot variance and compute constraints rather than copying CARL's counts mechanically.

### Citation

Benjamins et al. (2021), experimental methodology.

## E5 — In-distribution performance does not guarantee OOD generalization

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Experimental results
- **Claim:** Performance degrades as contexts move farther from the training distribution, and explicit context can help in some settings without providing a universal solution.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

CARL experiments show that increasing context variation can make training harder and that agents often behave best near the contexts represented during training. In out-of-distribution evaluations such as altered gravity conditions, performance can decline substantially as the test context moves farther from the training distribution.

### Context and limits

The quantitative findings depend on the specific agents and context distributions used in the paper.

### Thesis use

Define separate ID, near-OOD, and far-OOD test bands rather than reporting one generalization average.

### Citation

Benjamins et al. (2021), experimental results.

## E6 — CARL's reported experiments are static-context generalization tests, not continual recovery tests

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Discussion and future work
- **Claim:** The paper primarily evaluates fixed contexts and does not empirically establish continual adaptation to within-run dynamic context changes.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Although the CARL framework can expose many environmental factors, the reported study focuses on static contexts and largely varies individual context features. Dynamic contexts and continual-learning evaluations are identified as future directions rather than demonstrated results.

### Thesis use

Use CARL for static context/generalization design principles and keep changepoint/recovery experiments conceptually separate.

### Citation

Benjamins et al. (2021), discussion and future work.

## `SRC-4000D2B40A` — Behaviour Suite for Reinforcement Learning

- **Συγγραφείς:** Ian Osband; Yotam Doron; Matteo Hessel; John Aslanides; Eren Sezener; Andre Saraiva; Katrina McKinney; Tor Lattimore; Csaba Szepesvari; Satinder Singh; Benjamin Van Roy; Richard Sutton; David Silver; Hado Van Hasselt
- **Έτος:** 2019
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** GridWorld; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-4000D2B40A.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-4000D2B40A.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.03568)

## E1 — A diagnostic experiment includes environment, interaction regime, and analysis

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2
- **Claim:** bsuite defines each experiment through a fixed set of parameterized environments, a fixed agent–environment interaction regime, and a fixed analysis procedure.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Osband et al. state that a bsuite experiment contains three components: the environments, the interaction protocol, and the analysis that maps observed behavior to results and plots. The benchmark therefore standardizes more than the simulator itself.

### Thesis use

Version every resilience scenario together with its interaction budget, update rules, and analysis procedure; do not compare agents under different post-processing or budgets.

### Citation

Osband et al. (2019), Section 2.

## E2 — Diagnostic tests should be targeted, simple, challenging, scalable, and fast

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2, experiment-design criteria
- **Claim:** A good diagnostic isolates a specific capability, removes unnecessary confounds, stresses the agent, varies systematically in difficulty, and remains fast enough for iteration.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper lists five qualities for bsuite experiments: targeted, simple, challenging, scalable, and fast. The goal is to isolate one important RL capability and reveal how it scales, rather than to build one complicated environment whose many factors make failures hard to interpret.

### Thesis use

Use independent severity sweeps for observation noise, transition noise, reward changes, obstacles, and action failures before combining factors.

### Citation

Osband et al. (2019), Section 2.

## E3 — Standard comparison can remain behavior-based and architecture-independent

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2, behavioral measurement discussion
- **Claim:** bsuite standard analysis measures only properties observable through the environment, enabling comparison across algorithms and codebases without requiring internal-state access.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The suite deliberately bases its standard metrics on externally observable agent behavior. Internal beliefs, neural activations, and implementation-specific diagnostics may still be studied, but they are not required for the common benchmark score.

### Thesis use

Base cross-agent comparison on trajectories, returns, success, safety violations, interventions, and recovery. Keep neural or agent-specific diagnostics secondary.

### Citation

Osband et al. (2019), Section 2.

## E4 — Parameter sweeps expose scaling limits hidden by a single task instance

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2.1 and other parameterized experiment descriptions
- **Claim:** Varying a task parameter across a range reveals where a capability ceases to scale.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The memory-length experiment varies how many sequential steps an agent must retain information, and the suite uses similar parameterization for other capabilities. Performance over a sweep therefore provides a capability profile instead of a binary result on one fixed environment.

### Thesis use

Report severity–performance and severity–recovery curves for each perturbation under a fixed interaction budget.

### Citation

Osband et al. (2019), Section 2.1 and parameterized experiment descriptions.

## E5 — Aggregate scores are summaries, not substitutes for diagnostic curves

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 1.2 and 2
- **Claim:** bsuite provides normalized scores for quick comparison but explicitly positions itself as a diagnostic suite rather than a leaderboard.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Each experiment maps behavior to a score in `[0,1]` for at-a-glance comparison, while the accompanying analysis notebooks expose detailed plots and measurements. The authors explicitly state that bsuite is not intended to become a leaderboard to optimize at the expense of understanding agent behavior.

### Thesis use

If a composite resilience score is reported, always publish the underlying per-perturbation curves and uncertainty intervals.

### Citation

Osband et al. (2019), Sections 1.2 and 2.

## E6 — Diagnostic suites do not establish general intelligence or external validity

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 1.2–1.3
- **Claim:** The authors present bsuite as an evolving collection of targeted experiments, not a comprehensive substitute for larger benchmarks or proof of broad capability.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper explicitly says the suite does not capture all or even most important RL issues and should complement rather than replace large integrated challenge environments. Its purpose is to make particular capabilities concrete, measurable, and reproducible.

### Thesis use

Frame the GridWorld benchmark as a controlled diagnostic study whose conclusions are scoped to the tested perturbations and agents.

### Citation

Osband et al. (2019), Sections 1.2–1.3.

## `SRC-486FAE4C93` — Artificial Intelligence Risk Management Framework (AI RMF 1.0) NIST AI 100-1

- **Συγγραφείς:** National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST)
- **Έτος:** 2023
- **Τύπος:** θεσμική ή τεχνική αναφορά
- **Θέματα / tags:** ανθεκτικότητα και ανάκαμψη; διακυβέρνηση και ηθική
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-486FAE4C93.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-486FAE4C93.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)

## E1 — AI risk can change with data and deployment context
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Executive Summary, pp. 1–3
- **Claim:** AI systems may operate on data or in contexts that change over time, sometimes significantly and unexpectedly, affecting functionality and trustworthiness.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
NIST notes that AI systems can be trained on or exposed to data that change over time and that deployment contexts are often complex. Such changes can alter system behavior and trustworthiness in ways that are difficult to detect or respond to, motivating lifecycle-oriented risk management rather than a one-time pre-deployment assessment.

### Context and limits
The framework is broad institutional guidance. It does not define a reinforcement-learning changepoint detector, adaptation algorithm, or recovery metric.

### Thesis use
Use as institutional motivation for evaluating controlled post-deployment changes and for documenting monitoring assumptions.

### Citation
NIST (2023), AI RMF 1.0, Executive Summary.

## E2 — Trustworthiness is multidimensional
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3, pp. 12–17
- **Claim:** Validity and reliability, safety, security and resilience, accountability, explainability, privacy, and fairness are distinct trustworthiness characteristics.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The AI RMF presents valid and reliable behavior as foundational but separately discusses safety, secure and resilient operation, accountability and transparency, explainability and interpretability, privacy enhancement, and fairness with harmful bias managed. A system can therefore perform well on one characteristic without satisfying the others.

### Context and limits
These categories are intentionally general and do not supply task-specific thresholds for a GridWorld experiment.

### Thesis use
Avoid treating average return as a complete proxy for reliability, safety, or resilience; retain separate metric families.

### Citation
NIST (2023), Section 3.

## E3 — Failure to measure a risk is not evidence that the risk is absent
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 1.2.1, Risk Measurement
- **Claim:** Poorly defined or inadequately understood risks are difficult to measure, and the inability to measure them does not imply that the system is low-risk or high-risk.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
NIST emphasizes that risk measurement can fail because concepts are not well defined, reliable metrics are unavailable, methods do not transfer across contexts, or simplified metrics can be gamed or omit important nuance. Measurement limitations must therefore be documented rather than interpreted as evidence of safety.

### Context and limits
This principle does not justify avoiding quantitative evaluation when suitable technical measurements are available.

### Thesis use
Report metric assumptions and limitations and do not rely on a single composite resilience score without its component degradation, recovery, and safety measures.

### Citation
NIST (2023), Section 1.2.1.

## E4 — GOVERN, MAP, MEASURE, and MANAGE form a lifecycle process
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Part 2, Section 5, pp. 20–33
- **Claim:** The AI RMF organizes risk-management activities into interacting GOVERN, MAP, MEASURE, and MANAGE functions applied throughout the system lifecycle.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
GOVERN establishes cross-cutting policies and responsibilities, MAP connects the system to its deployment context and potential risks, MEASURE evaluates system behavior and trustworthiness, and MANAGE prioritizes and responds to identified risks. These functions are designed to interact and recur rather than act as a single sequential checklist.

### Context and limits
A thesis benchmark can borrow documentation and monitoring principles from this structure but should not claim organizational compliance with the AI RMF.

### Thesis use
Structure the experimental record around explicit assumptions, mapped perturbation scenarios, measured effects, and documented response mechanisms.

### Citation
NIST (2023), Part 2, Section 5.

## E5 — “Resilient” in the AI RMF should not be substituted for the thesis's operational recovery definition
- **Type:** scope clarification grounded in the framework
- **Location:** Section 3.3, Secure and Resilient
- **Claim:** NIST uses resilience within a broad trustworthiness and security context; a reinforcement-learning experiment still needs a precise operational definition of post-disruption performance and recovery.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The AI RMF groups secure and resilient characteristics with protection against adverse events and the ability to withstand or recover from them. This broad framing is useful context but does not define the exact temporal quantities needed to compare learning agents after controlled environmental shifts.

### Thesis use
Use NIST only as high-level motivation and retain the thesis-specific metrics for immediate degradation, recovery time, recovered level, repeated-change behavior, and safety cost.

### Citation
NIST (2023), Section 3.3.

## `SRC-4B456A9363` — https---proceedings.mlr.press-v202-feng23e-feng23e.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2018
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** μη στασιμότητα; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-4B456A9363.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-4B456A9363.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v202/feng23e/feng23e.pdf)

## E1 — Sliding windows reduce mismatch between stale samples and the current MDP

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1
- **Claim:** SW-OPEA constructs its confidence set from recent episodes and accounts for local reward/transition variation so current-value estimates are not dominated by obsolete data.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Feng et al. design a confidence-set model-free learner whose non-stationary component is a sliding window. The confidence construction incorporates local variation across the episodes retained in that window so the current optimal value function remains plausible despite distribution mismatch between earlier and current MDPs.

### Context and limits

The theoretical method uses general function approximation and is substantially more complex than a simple tabular recency heuristic.

### Thesis use

Use the source as justification for a recent-memory baseline, not as a requirement to reproduce SW-OPEA itself.

### Citation

Feng et al. (2023), Abstract and Section 1.

## E2 — Sliding window, restart, and exponential weighting are different non-stationarity mechanisms

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 1.1
- **Claim:** The related literature handles stale data through finite windows, learner restarts, or gradual exponential down-weighting of older experience.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper distinguishes several algorithmic families: sliding-window approaches in tabular non-stationary MDPs, restart schemes for accumulated change, and exponentially weighted least-squares methods that continuously reduce the influence of older observations.

### Context and limits

These methods are developed under different structural assumptions and regret analyses and should not be treated as interchangeable.

### Thesis use

Retain separate baselines for full reset, finite recent window, and exponential recency/decay.

### Citation

Feng et al. (2023), Section 1.1.

## E3 — Window length creates a bias–uncertainty trade-off

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Theorem 5.2; Corollary 5.3
- **Claim:** The regret bound depends jointly on window length and environmental variation, so no single window is optimal for all rates of change.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

A shorter window reduces contamination from older regimes but uses fewer samples for estimation, whereas a longer window provides more data while increasing mismatch when rewards or transitions have changed. The theoretical choice of window length is tied to variation quantities in the environment.

### Context and limits

The theory does not provide a universal window size for a new GridWorld, and a budget-aware choice can itself be oracle-like if the true variation is assumed known.

### Thesis use

Tune effective memory length on validation shifts and report sensitivity rather than selecting it from final test results.

### Citation

Feng et al. (2023), Theorem 5.2 and Corollary 5.3.

## E4 — Dynamic regret contains both stationary-learning and non-stationarity costs

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Discussion following Corollary 5.3
- **Claim:** The theoretical bound separates a component associated with learning a stationary problem from additional terms caused by changes in rewards and transitions.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The analysis decomposes the dynamic-regret guarantee into a term analogous to the cost of learning a static MDP and extra penalties introduced by non-stationary reward and transition variation. This makes the adaptation burden conceptually distinct from baseline learning difficulty.

### Thesis use

Report clean learning performance separately from immediate degradation, recovery deficit, and adaptation overhead.

### Citation

Feng et al. (2023), discussion after Corollary 5.3.

## E5 — SW-OPEA is most attractive in relatively small-variation regimes

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 5–6
- **Claim:** The paper's theoretical comparisons indicate that SW-OPEA improves several dependencies but is principally advantageous when environmental variation is not too large.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

In linear and tabular special cases, the derived bound improves some dimension and horizon dependencies while having a less favorable dependence on average transition variation. The authors therefore characterize the method as efficient when the non-stationarity budget is comparatively small.

### Context and limits

These are comparisons of theoretical upper bounds, not empirical rankings from a common benchmark.

### Thesis use

Use recency-based baselines primarily for gradual or moderate change and retain reset-style comparators for severe abrupt shifts.

### Citation

Feng et al. (2023), Sections 5–6.

## `SRC-4D2B7DDC38` — Distributionally Robust Reinforcement Learning with Interactive Data Collection: Fundamental Hardness and Near-Optimal Algorithms

- **Συγγραφείς:** Miao Lu; Han Zhong; Tong Zhang; Jose Blanchet
- **Έτος:** 2024
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** εύρωστη ενισχυτική μάθηση; ανθεκτικότητα και ανάκαμψη; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; στατιστική αξιολόγηση; πράκτορες τεχνητής νοημοσύνης
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-4D2B7DDC38.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-4D2B7DDC38.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03578)

## E1 — Interactive robust RL can be fundamentally hard under support shift
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction; Section 3
- **Claim:** Without additional assumptions, sample-efficient robust learning from interaction with only the training environment can be impossible when testing environments contain important states or transitions outside the support accessible during training.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Lu et al. show a fundamental gap between robust RL with interactive training data and settings that provide a generative model or an offline dataset with deployment coverage. If states that matter under plausible test dynamics cannot be reached in the training MDP, no amount of ordinary exploration in that training environment can directly supply evidence about them. Their lower bound formalizes this curse of support shift.

### Thesis use
Separate in-support parameter variation from low-probability reachable transitions and genuinely out-of-support structural shifts when interpreting failures.

### Citation
Lu et al. (2024/2026 revision), Abstract, Introduction, and Section 3.

## E2 — More exploration cannot reveal states forbidden by the training dynamics
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Introduction
- **Claim:** Sophisticated exploration does not solve a coverage gap when crucial test states are structurally inaccessible under the training environment.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The paper emphasizes that interactive data collection cannot force the training environment to generate a transition or state that its dynamics never permit. By contrast, a generative-model oracle can query arbitrary state–action pairs, and a suitably covered offline dataset may already contain deployment-relevant transitions.

### Thesis use
Do not interpret failure on a held-out structural layout as weak exploration unless the relevant post-shift states were actually reachable during training.

### Citation
Lu et al. (2024/2026 revision), Introduction.

## E3 — Data-access assumptions determine what robustness guarantees are comparable
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction
- **Claim:** Generative-model access, well-covered offline data, and ordinary interaction with a training environment are materially different information regimes.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The authors explicitly distinguish a generative model that can return a next-state sample for any queried state–action pair from an ordinary simulator/training environment whose data distribution is induced by the learner's policy. They also distinguish both from a pre-collected offline dataset with favorable coverage.

### Thesis use
Track simulator queries, offline samples, and ordinary interactions separately and reject algorithm rankings built on unmatched coverage privileges.

### Citation
Lu et al. (2024/2026 revision), Introduction.

## E4 — Near-optimal learning requires an additional structural assumption in the TV-robust case
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 4.1
- **Claim:** The paper restores tractability for a subclass of total-variation robust MDPs using a vanishing-minimal-value assumption that removes the support-shift pathology.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
After establishing the general impossibility result, the authors identify a restricted class in which the minimal value of the optimal robust value function vanishes. Under this assumption for a total-variation ambiguity set, they design OPROVI-TV and derive near-optimal sample-complexity guarantees.

### Context and limits
This assumption is part of the theorem. The resulting guarantee should not be presented as a generic solution to arbitrary sim-to-real or structural-shift problems.

### Thesis use
Use the theorem primarily as a warning that robust guarantees are conditional on coverage and structural assumptions.

### Citation
Lu et al. (2024/2026 revision), Abstract and Section 4.1.

## E5 — Robustness to an ambiguity set remains distinct from temporal recovery
- **Type:** scope synthesis grounded in the formulation
- **Location:** Sections 1–4
- **Claim:** The paper studies worst-case performance over a predefined family of test environments and online data collection in the training environment; it does not define an unknown deployment changepoint followed by detection and relearning.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Classify this source under `robust_model_shift/coverage`, not as direct evidence for detector-triggered resilience.

### Citation
Lu et al. (2024/2026 revision), overall formulation.

## `SRC-4E5300CD15` — Online Robust Planning Under Model Uncertainty: A Sample-Based Approach

- **Συγγραφείς:** Tamir Shazman, Idan Lev-Yehudi, Ron Benchetrit, Vadim Indelman
- **Έτος:** 1987
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** εύρωστη ενισχυτική μάθηση; αβεβαιότητα μεταβάσεων; μερική παρατηρησιμότητα
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-4E5300CD15.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-4E5300CD15.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/view/40959/44920)

## Evidence E1 — RSS is robust online planning under model misspecification
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Sections 1–1.1
- **Claim:** Robust Sparse Sampling extends online sample-based planning to robust MDPs by optimizing a robust value under uncertainty in the transition model.
- **Thesis use:** robust-planning comparator
- **Topics:** robust MDP; online planning; model misspecification
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
RSS plans from the current state using samples from an approximate generative model, but unlike nominal Sparse Sampling it evaluates actions against a set of plausible transition models and optimizes for the worst case within that set. The method is therefore a robustness mechanism for imperfect dynamics models rather than a model-free continual learner.

## Evidence E2 — The uncertainty model assumes a bounded transition-model error
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2.1, Equations (1)–(3)
- **Claim:** The true transition kernel is assumed to lie within a Total-Variation radius around the approximate model, with a rectangular uncertainty-set structure.
- **Thesis use:** uncertainty-model disclosure
- **Topics:** Total Variation; uncertainty radius; rectangularity
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
For each state-action pair, the paper assumes that the distance between the true transition distribution and the approximate generative model is bounded by a radius ρ. The robust MDP uses the common rectangularity assumption, so uncertainty is modeled independently across state-action pairs.

### Limitation
The radius and uncertainty-set structure are assumptions or inputs. They should not be confused with an online changepoint estimate.

## Evidence E3 — Robust planning has finite-sample guarantees but still incurs planning cost
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 1.1; theoretical analysis
- **Claim:** RSS provides a finite-sample value approximation guarantee whose complexity is independent of the size of the state space.
- **Thesis use:** computational trade-off reporting
- **Topics:** finite-sample guarantee; lookahead; samples
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The paper derives an error bound that can be reduced by choosing sufficient planning depth and sample width. Independence from state-space size does not imply negligible runtime: online lookahead still depends on planning horizon, action branching, sample width, and the robust backup computation.

## Evidence E4 — Robustness can improve performance as model error grows
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** experiments, including stochastic FrozenLake
- **Claim:** RSS is reported to outperform nominal Sparse Sampling when the approximate transition model is sufficiently misspecified and to reduce catastrophic failures in the tested settings.
- **Thesis use:** controlled model-error scenario
- **Topics:** FrozenLake; model uncertainty; failure rate
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
In the reported stochastic navigation experiment, nominal planning with an erroneous model degrades as transition-model uncertainty increases, whereas RSS maintains stronger return and failure behavior over the tested uncertainty levels. A planner using the true dynamics remains an oracle-style upper reference.

### Limitation
This is model misspecification under a specified uncertainty set, not an unknown temporal changepoint.

## Evidence E5 — Robustness may trade nominal performance for protection
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** empirical results and discussion
- **Claim:** Worst-case planning can be conservative when model error is small, while becoming more advantageous as misspecification increases.
- **Thesis use:** conservativeness reporting
- **Topics:** nominal utility; disturbed utility; safety trade-off
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Optimizing against adverse transition models can sacrifice some nominal performance when the approximate model is already accurate. The value of the robust planner becomes clearer as model error increases, so evaluation should report both clean/low-error utility and performance under misspecification.

## Evidence E6 — RSS is not a change detector or a continued-learning mechanism
- **Type:** thesis-safe inference from the formulation
- **Location:** Sections 2–6
- **Claim:** RSS plans with a predeclared model-uncertainty set and does not introduce changepoint detection, regime memory, or online relearning across repeated environmental changes.
- **Thesis use:** mechanism taxonomy
- **Topics:** robustness; adaptation boundary; detector
- **Status:** verified

## Avoid overclaiming
This source supports a model-based robustness comparator. It does not establish online resilience to unannounced regime changes, and a known uncertainty radius should be reported as prior/oracle information when used in experiments.

## `SRC-52E62452B8` — https---people.eecs.berkeley.edu-~elghaoui-Pubs-RobMDP OR2005.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2005
- **Τύπος:** ιστοσελίδα
- **Θέματα / tags:** αβεβαιότητα μεταβάσεων; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; στατιστική αξιολόγηση
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-52E62452B8.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-52E62452B8.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~elghaoui/Pubs/RobMDP_OR2005.pdf)

## E1 — Nominal optimal policies can be sensitive to transition-probability estimation error
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction
- **Claim:** Errors in estimated transition matrices can materially change the behavior and performance of an MDP policy, motivating explicit robust control when the transition model is uncertain.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Nilim and El Ghaoui motivate robust MDP control by observing that transition probabilities often have to be estimated from limited data and that nominal MDP solutions can be highly sensitive to those estimates. Their robust formulation therefore optimizes against a set of plausible transition matrices rather than treating one estimated matrix as exact.

### Thesis use
Use as foundational support for a static robust baseline under transition-model uncertainty.

### Citation
Nilim and El Ghaoui (2005), Abstract and Introduction.

## E2 — Rectangular uncertainty enables robust dynamic programming
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2.2; Section 3.1
- **Claim:** Under the paper's rectangular uncertainty property, the time-varying robust-control game admits a Bellman-style robust dynamic-programming recursion.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The uncertainty set is factorized across rows of the transition matrices, so nature can choose a next-state distribution from the local admissible set associated with the current state and action. The resulting robust Bellman recursion replaces the nominal expected continuation value with the worst continuation value over that local set.

### Context and limits
Rectangularity excludes correlations among uncertain transition rows/actions and is a structural tractability assumption.

### Thesis use
State uncertainty-set factorization explicitly rather than referring generically to “robust MDP.”

### Citation
Nilim and El Ghaoui (2005), Sections 2.2 and 3.1.

## E3 — Stationary and time-varying uncertainty models are different
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2.2
- **Claim:** The paper distinguishes an unknown transition model chosen once and then held fixed from a stronger model in which transition matrices may vary over time within the prescribed bounds.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
In the stationary uncertainty model, nature selects transition matrices that remain fixed for the horizon. In the time-varying model, admissible transition matrices may change between stages. The time-varying formulation is easier to solve with robust dynamic programming but represents a different uncertainty assumption.

### Thesis use
Do not equate time-varying robust uncertainty with an unknown piecewise-stationary changepoint process; state which temporal uncertainty model is being evaluated.

### Citation
Nilim and El Ghaoui (2005), Section 2.2.

## E4 — Statistical quality of the uncertainty set affects conservativeness and tractability
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction; Sections 5–7
- **Claim:** Some generic polytopic or interval uncertainty sets can be poor statistical representations and produce overly conservative robust policies; likelihood- or entropy-based sets can provide a more meaningful alternative in the paper's setting.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The authors argue that tractability alone is not enough when choosing an uncertainty set. Broad interval-style sets can include unrealistic transition models and induce excessive pessimism. They develop uncertainty models based on likelihood regions or entropy bounds that retain statistical meaning while keeping the inner robust recursion computationally manageable.

### Thesis use
Report uncertainty-set family, calibration procedure, radius, and nominal-performance loss together.

### Citation
Nilim and El Ghaoui (2005), Abstract, Introduction, and Sections 5–7.

## E5 — Robust planning is not online resilience
- **Type:** scope synthesis grounded in the formulation
- **Location:** Overall paper
- **Claim:** The robust policy is optimized for an uncertainty model; the paper does not define an unknown changepoint detector, context recall, or a continued-learning recovery process.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Keep `static_robust_policy` and `adaptive_post_change_policy` as different agent categories and test out-of-set structural shifts separately from in-set model uncertainty.

### Citation
Nilim and El Ghaoui (2005), overall formulation.

## `SRC-5775601BD7` — https---proceedings.mlr.press-v232-abbas23a-abbas23a.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2022
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** ανθεκτικότητα και ανάκαμψη; μη στασιμότητα; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-5775601BD7.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-5775601BD7.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v232/abbas23a/abbas23a.pdf)

## Evidence E1 — Switching ALE evaluates repeated regime changes without resetting the learner
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 1 and 3.1
- **Claim:** A single deep-RL agent cycles through a fixed sequence of Atari games while retaining its learned parameters and replay state across environment switches.
- **Thesis use:** repeated-change protocol
- **Topics:** continual learning; repeated visits; no reset
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Switching ALE changes the environment by moving one agent through a repeated sequence of games. The learner spends a fixed interaction budget in each game and switches without resetting the neural network weights or replay buffer, allowing later visits to reveal whether the same learner can continue to acquire competence over a long sequence of non-stationary regimes.

### Limitation
Changing the Atari game alters many aspects of the task simultaneously and is more severe than a single-axis transition or reward perturbation.

## Evidence E2 — Relearning ability can deteriorate on successive visits
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3.2; Figure 2
- **Claim:** Rainbow often learns more slowly and reaches lower performance each time it revisits the same game.
- **Thesis use:** per-occurrence recovery metrics
- **Topics:** relearning; plasticity; repeated recovery
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
In Switching ALE, the agent can perform well during its first exposure to a game and then show progressively poorer learning on later visits. The reported curves show slower learning and lower end-of-visit performance, eventually reaching visits with little sign of improvement.

### Thesis-safe implication
Recovery should be indexed by occurrence number in repeated-change experiments rather than averaged into a single post-change score.

## Evidence E3 — A reset-from-scratch agent is a plasticity diagnostic
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3.2; Figure 2
- **Claim:** The paper uses an idealized reset agent to represent how a fresh learner would relearn each game if prior tasks neither helped nor harmed its ability to learn.
- **Thesis use:** reset comparator
- **Topics:** reset; relearning capacity; interference
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The reset comparator discards previous weights, replay contents, and optimizer state so that every visit corresponds to fresh learning. It forgets all prior competence, but it preserves the ability to reach approximately the same scratch-learning performance on every visit. A continual learner that falls below this reference can therefore be diagnosed as having lost part of its ability to learn, not merely as having forgotten a previously stored solution.

### Limitation
The reset agent is a diagnostic comparator, not an inherently resilient strategy.

## Evidence E4 — Loss of plasticity has neural signatures in deep value-based agents
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 4–6 and Conclusion
- **Claim:** The analysis links deteriorating continual learning with increasingly sparse activation patterns and diminishing gradients.
- **Thesis use:** optional deep-agent diagnostics
- **Topics:** activations; gradients; neural plasticity
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
As continual training proceeds, the paper observes changes in network activations and gradients that accompany the loss of learning capacity. A simple activation-function change, Concatenated ReLU, mitigates some of this plasticity loss in the studied deep value-based agents.

### Limitation
These diagnostics are specific to neural agents and should not be used to explain slow recovery in tabular Q-learning.

## Evidence E5 — Preserving plasticity does not eliminate interference
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** later experiments, including the CReLU analysis
- **Claim:** Improving the network's ability to keep learning does not automatically prevent destructive interference from previous tasks.
- **Thesis use:** retention/plasticity distinction
- **Topics:** plasticity; catastrophic interference; retention
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The CReLU intervention can improve continued learning capacity while gaps relative to uninterrupted single-game training still remain or grow in some games. Plasticity and interference are therefore related but distinct failure modes: an agent may remain capable of updating while still carrying harmful effects from previous learning.

## Evidence E6 — Very expensive continual experiments constrain statistical certainty
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** experimental discussion and footnotes around Section 3.2
- **Claim:** Some experiments span extremely large interaction budgets and are averaged over a small number of runs because of computational cost.
- **Thesis use:** uncertainty/reporting discipline
- **Topics:** compute budget; seeds; statistical uncertainty
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The paper reports experiments lasting up to very large numbers of environment interactions and substantial wall-clock time. The authors consequently treat some results as demonstrative rather than as proof that the same failure must occur in every deep continual-learning configuration.

## Avoid overclaiming
Loss of plasticity, catastrophic forgetting, and negative transfer should not be treated as synonyms. The source supports a deep-agent plasticity diagnosis and repeated-regime protocol; it does not imply that neural mitigations such as CReLU belong in the tabular thesis core.

## `SRC-5E062C81BA` — https---proceedings.nips.cc-paper files-paper-2000-file-e8dfff4676a47048d6f0c4ef899593dd-Paper.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2000
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** εύρωστη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-5E062C81BA.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-5E062C81BA.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://proceedings.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2000/file/e8dfff4676a47048d6f0c4ef899593dd-Paper.pdf)

## E1 — Robust RL is formulated as an actor–disturber minimax problem
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Sections 2–3
- **Claim:** The paper adapts `H-infinity` control ideas to reinforcement learning by introducing a disturbing agent that seeks adverse inputs while the control agent seeks the best response.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Morimoto and Doya reinterpret modeling error as an adverse disturbance and formulate policy learning as a differential game. The actor optimizes control while a disturber selects the most damaging input allowed by the robustness criterion, producing a minimax value objective rather than ordinary expected-return maximization.

### Thesis use
Use as a historical primary source for the static/worst-case robust-policy category.

### Citation
Morimoto and Doya (2000), Abstract and Sections 2–3.

## E2 — The robustness criterion defines the disturbance class
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 2–3
- **Claim:** The `H-infinity`-inspired objective penalizes disturbance magnitude while requiring the controller to perform well against the corresponding worst disturbance.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The robust value function combines task/control performance with a term involving disturbance magnitude. The robustness parameter determines which disturbance-to-error gains the controller is designed to tolerate, so robustness is always relative to a specified disturbance model rather than an unrestricted notion of “any change.”

### Thesis use
State the perturbation/threat model explicitly for every robust baseline.

### Citation
Morimoto and Doya (2000), Sections 2–3.

## E3 — The nonlinear pendulum case study shows zero-update robustness to parameter changes
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4.2; Figure 4
- **Claim:** A controller trained with the robust objective continued to swing up the pendulum after changing mass and friction, whereas the nominal comparator failed in that reported configuration.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Both controllers are trained under the nominal pendulum parameters. The paper then changes physical parameters including mass and friction and reports that the robust controller retains successful swing-up behavior while the standard model-based RL controller does not. The robustness is a property of the already learned controller; no separate change alarm or post-change retraining step is required for this comparison.

### Context and limits
This is a single continuous-control case study and should not be generalized to arbitrary structural changes or tabular environments.

### Thesis use
Measure frozen-policy post-change performance before enabling online updates, so immediate robustness is not attributed to adaptation.

### Citation
Morimoto and Doya (2000), Section 4.2 and Figure 4.

## E4 — Robustness can trade nominal peak performance for tolerance to mismatch
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4.2
- **Claim:** In the nominal pendulum condition, the standard controller can complete the task with fewer swings, while the robust controller is designed for stronger tolerance to model variation.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Report clean/nominal return together with disturbed return and a conservativeness cost for robust agents.

### Citation
Morimoto and Doya (2000), Section 4.2.

## E5 — Worst-case disturbance training is not changepoint adaptation
- **Type:** scope synthesis grounded in the paper
- **Location:** Overall formulation and experiments
- **Claim:** The method has no explicit changepoint detector, recurring-context memory, or repeated-regime recovery metric.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Keep `frozen_robustness` and `online_post_shift_adaptation` as separate evaluation phases and agent categories.

### Citation
Morimoto and Doya (2000), overall formulation.

## `SRC-6126015212` — https---proceedings.mlr.press-v119-wachi20a-wachi20a.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2020
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** ασφαλής ενισχυτική μάθηση; στατιστική αξιολόγηση
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-6126015212.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-6126015212.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v119/wachi20a/wachi20a.pdf)

## E1 — The safety function can be unknown and learned during safe exploration
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction; Section 2
- **Claim:** SNO-MDP studies an MDP in which both reward and a state-based safety function are initially unknown, while every visited state must satisfy a safety threshold.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Wachi and Sui formulate safe reinforcement learning with an unknown safety function `g(s)` and require the agent to remain in states whose safety value is above a threshold. The key difficulty is that the learner must collect information about safety without freely entering states whose safety has not been established.

### Context and limits
The theoretical results rely on prior safe states and regularity assumptions for the unknown functions, including kernel/RKHS and Lipschitz structure.

### Thesis use
Distinguish methods that know the hazard map in advance from methods that spend data and compute learning safety information.

### Citation
Wachi and Sui (2020), Abstract, Introduction, and Section 2.

## E2 — A pessimistic safe set includes reachability and returnability, not only local state labels
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3, Predicted pessimistic safe space
- **Claim:** States admitted to the certified pessimistic safe region must be judged safe with high probability and also be reachable from and returnable to the previously certified region.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The paper first forms a set of states whose lower confidence bounds satisfy the safety threshold. It then restricts this set using reachability and returnability operators. A locally safe-looking state is therefore not enough: the agent must be able to enter it through an allowed transition and retain a safe route back to the established safe set.

### Thesis use
For blocked-corridor or topology changes, recompute safe connectivity rather than assuming unchanged per-cell hazard labels imply unchanged safety.

### Citation
Wachi and Sui (2020), Section 3.

## E3 — The optimistic and pessimistic safe sets serve different purposes
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3, Predicted optimistic safe space
- **Claim:** The pessimistic set contains states certified safe at the chosen confidence level, whereas the optimistic set includes states that may still prove safe and can guide information gathering.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The optimistic set is built from upper confidence information and includes states that remain plausible candidates for safety, even when they are not yet certified by the lower bound. The pessimistic set is the region in which the safety guarantee is maintained. This creates an explicit distinction between states that are safe enough to use and states that are merely promising to investigate.

### Thesis use
Do not collapse `uncertain-but-potentially-safe` and `certified-safe` states into one label when evaluating safety exploration.

### Citation
Wachi and Sui (2020), Section 3.

## E4 — SNO-MDP separates safety exploration from reward optimization
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction; algorithm overview
- **Claim:** The algorithm first expands or learns the certified safe region and then optimizes cumulative reward within the safe region.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
SNO-MDP uses a stepwise procedure rather than optimizing reward and safety discovery as one undifferentiated objective. The first phase gathers enough information to construct an appropriate safe region. The second phase performs reward-oriented learning subject to the certified safety restriction.

### Context and limits
This separation incurs an opportunity cost because interactions spent learning safety are not directly optimized for task reward.

### Thesis use
Report safety-exploration steps, task-return opportunity cost, and the delay before reward-focused adaptation resumes.

### Citation
Wachi and Sui (2020), Abstract and Introduction.

## E5 — Early stopping limits unnecessary safety exploration
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Introduction; ES2 contribution and later algorithm analysis
- **Claim:** The ES2 mechanism is designed to stop safety-region exploration once sufficient information has been collected for near-optimal reward behavior, rather than expanding the safe region indefinitely.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The authors introduce Early Stopping of Exploration of Safety to reduce the amount of exploration devoted to safety while preserving probabilistic safety and near-optimality guarantees under their assumptions. The motivation is that complete knowledge of every potentially safe state is unnecessary when additional safety information would not materially improve the achievable reward policy.

### Thesis use
If a safety-learning baseline is included, distinguish information needed for safe task execution from exhaustive hazard-map learning.

### Citation
Wachi and Sui (2020), Introduction and ES2 analysis.

## E6 — The safety machinery is not a generic changepoint detector
- **Type:** scope synthesis grounded in the paper
- **Location:** Overall formulation
- **Claim:** SNO-MDP learns initially unknown reward and safety functions under a fixed structured model; it does not primarily detect temporal changes in the environment and then trigger post-shift relearning.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Use SNO-MDP as background for unknown-safety exploration and safe-set connectivity, not as evidence that a GP safety model detects arbitrary environmental shifts.

### Citation
Wachi and Sui (2020), overall formulation.

## `SRC-620F17076C` — Robust Deep Reinforcement Learning against Adversarial Perturbations on State Observations

- **Συγγραφείς:** Huan Zhang; Hongge Chen; Chaowei Xiao; Bo Li; Mingyan Liu; Duane Boning; Cho-Jui Hsieh
- **Έτος:** 2020
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** εύρωστη ενισχυτική μάθηση; μη στασιμότητα; αβεβαιότητα παρατηρήσεων; μερική παρατηρησιμότητα; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; πράκτορες τεχνητής νοημοσύνης
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-620F17076C.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-620F17076C.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.08938)

## E1 — Observation corruption changes the policy input, not the true environment state

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 1; Figure 1; Section 3.1
- **Claim:** In the state-adversarial MDP, the agent acts from a perturbed observation while the environment transition still originates from the true state.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Zhang et al. model a state-observation adversary that replaces the agent's observation of true state `s` with an allowed perturbed observation `nu(s)`. The policy chooses its action from `nu(s)`, but the transition kernel evolves the environment from the underlying true state `s` using the selected action.

### Context and limits

This is an observation-uncertainty model. It should not be conflated with transition-dynamics changes or action-execution failures.

### Thesis use

Log true state and observed state separately in observation-corruption scenarios.

### Citation

Zhang et al. (2020), Section 1, Figure 1, and Section 3.1.

## E2 — Worst-case observation perturbation is different from a fixed stochastic observation model

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 1; Section 3.1
- **Claim:** The SA-MDP represents adversarial worst-case observation choice within an allowed set rather than a fixed conditional observation distribution.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper motivates SA-MDP because a standard POMDP observation kernel does not encode an adversary that deliberately selects an allowed observation to reduce return. The adversarial formulation therefore addresses a different uncertainty model from ordinary non-adversarial sensor noise.

### Context and limits

For stochastic measurement noise without an attacker, a probabilistic observation model or POMDP may be more appropriate.

### Thesis use

Label adversarial observation attacks separately from stochastic observation corruption.

### Citation

Zhang et al. (2020), Section 1 and Section 3.1.

## E3 — The robust control problem does not inherit all ordinary-MDP optimality properties

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3.1, Theorems 1–4
- **Claim:** Bellman evaluation is available for fixed policy/adversary combinations, but an optimal stationary Markov policy need not exist under the optimal observation adversary.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

For fixed policy and adversary, the induced system can be evaluated with Bellman equations. When optimizing against the worst observation adversary, however, the paper shows that the standard existence properties of ordinary MDPs do not transfer automatically. This motivates a constrained policy-improvement approach rather than naively reusing supervised adversarial-training ideas.

### Thesis use

State the exact robustness assumptions of any observation-robust baseline rather than treating it as an ordinary MDP with noise added informally.

### Citation

Zhang et al. (2020), Section 3.1, Theorems 1–4.

## E4 — Local policy smoothness provides a principled robustness surrogate

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3.2; Theorem 5
- **Claim:** The paper regularizes policies so action distributions change little within an allowed neighborhood of perturbed observations.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The proposed robustness objective controls the difference between the policy at the true state and policies evaluated at nearby admissible observations. The paper connects this requirement to total-variation and KL-style distances and derives algorithm-specific practical regularizers.

### Context and limits

The perturbation neighborhood must have semantic meaning for the chosen state representation.

### Thesis use

A simple tabular analogue can measure action disagreement or policy variation under bounded observation corruption.

### Citation

Zhang et al. (2020), Section 3.2 and Theorem 5.

## E5 — Clean and attacked performance should be reported together

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 4; Tables 1–3
- **Claim:** The empirical evaluation reports natural performance separately from performance under observation attacks.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The experiments compare agents under unperturbed execution and under several white-box observation attacks, and in some cases also report action-certification behavior. This exposes whether robustness gains under attack are accompanied by changes in nominal performance.

### Context and limits

Adversarial attack results do not directly imply robustness to ordinary stochastic noise, and the paper's algorithm ranking should not be transferred to a different benchmark.

### Thesis use

Report nominal return, perturbed return, and policy/action disagreement separately.

### Citation

Zhang et al. (2020), Section 4 and Tables 1–3.

## E6 — This is pre-emptive robustness, not online recovery

- **Type:** scope inference grounded in the paper
- **Location:** Sections 1–4
- **Claim:** The robust policies are trained before test-time attacks; the paper does not study unknown changepoints, detection, or continued post-change learning.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The contribution improves policies so they are less sensitive to bounded observation perturbations at evaluation time. It does not define a detector or an adaptation trajectory after an environmental regime change.

### Thesis use

Keep observation robustness separate from resilience metrics such as detection delay and recovery time.

### Citation

Zhang et al. (2020), overall method scope, Sections 1–4.

## `SRC-62996FD690` — Uncertainty-Based Out-of-Distribution Detection in Deep Reinforcement Learning

- **Συγγραφείς:** Andreas Sedlmeier; Thomas Gabor; Thomy Phan; Lenz Belzner; Claudia Linnhoff-Popien
- **Έτος:** 2019
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** GridWorld; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; στατιστική αξιολόγηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-62996FD690.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-62996FD690.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.02219)

## E1 — Epistemic uncertainty can act as an OOD signal

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Section 1
- **Claim:** The paper uses epistemic uncertainty from a value-estimation network to distinguish states that are poorly represented by training data.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Sedlmeier et al. motivate their detector through the relationship between epistemic uncertainty and lack of data. In their value-based RL setting, states from low-density or previously unseen regions are expected to produce higher epistemic uncertainty, so the uncertainty estimate can be used as a score for separating in-distribution and out-of-distribution observations.

### Context and limits

The method is evaluated with deep value networks. An uncertainty score is not automatically a calibrated probability of OOD membership and is not a sequential changepoint detector by itself.

### Thesis use

Use this source only to motivate an optional uncertainty-based detection signal whose calibration, false-alarm rate, and temporal detection behavior are evaluated separately.

### Citation

Sedlmeier et al. (2019), Abstract and Section 1.

## E2 — A controlled GridWorld can create a leakage-safe held-out OOD region

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3, Experimental Setup
- **Claim:** The study creates OOD states by reversing start and goal regions after training and keeps evaluation trajectories out of the training data.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The agent is trained in a 12×4 gridworld where starts are sampled from one half and goals from the other. For OOD evaluation, the start and goal regions are mirrored so the agent encounters states that were not visited during training. Evaluation runs use frozen model snapshots and their observations are not fed back into learning.

### Context and limits

This is a static train/test distribution shift. It does not measure adaptation or recovery after an online environmental changepoint.

### Thesis use

Use held-out structural configurations for leakage-safe OOD diagnostics and keep them separate from the online adaptation protocol.

### Citation

Sedlmeier et al. (2019), Section 3.

## E3 — Bootstrap-based uncertainty was more stable than dropout-based uncertainty in the studied task

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 4–5; Figure 1
- **Claim:** Bootstrap ensemble variants maintained a more reliable separation between training and mirror environments than the tested Monte-Carlo dropout variants.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Across training snapshots, the BOOT and BOOTP architectures generally assign higher epistemic uncertainty to the mirror environment than to the training environment, while the Monte-Carlo dropout variants show reversals in the relative uncertainty of the two environments. Adding the randomized prior increases the separation in the reported experiment.

### Context and limits

The result is specific to the tested architecture, gridworld, and training process. It does not establish universal superiority of bootstrap ensembles over dropout or other uncertainty estimators.

### Thesis use

Select any uncertainty estimator through held-out calibration and sequential detection tests rather than theoretical popularity alone.

### Citation

Sedlmeier et al. (2019), Sections 4–5 and Figure 1.

## E4 — OOD detection and environmental change detection remain different problems

- **Type:** scope inference grounded in the paper
- **Location:** Sections 1–5
- **Claim:** The paper classifies frozen evaluation states as in- or out-of-distribution; it does not infer the time of a persistent MDP regime change or specify a recovery action.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The proposed uncertainty score is evaluated by comparing observations from a training environment with observations from a separate mirror environment. There is no unknown within-stream changepoint, detector-to-adapter interface, or policy-recovery protocol in the study.

### Thesis use

Do not equate high epistemic uncertainty or static OOD classification with successful changepoint detection or resilience.

### Citation

Sedlmeier et al. (2019), overall method scope, Sections 1–5.

## `SRC-630F83DAD7` — Leveraging Procedural Generation to Benchmark Reinforcement Learning

- **Συγγραφείς:** Karl Cobbe; Christopher Hesse; Jacob Hilton; John Schulman
- **Έτος:** 2019
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; περιβάλλοντα και benchmarks
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-630F83DAD7.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-630F83DAD7.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.01588)

## E1 — Procedural diversity is part of the benchmark distribution
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Abstract; Introduction; Section 2
- **Claim:** Procgen uses procedurally generated level distributions so agents encounter substantial environment variation rather than repeatedly training on near-identical states.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
Cobbe et al. design Procgen so layout, entities, assets, and other task details vary across generated levels. The resulting diversity is an environment property used to train and evaluate generalization; it is not merely an augmentation applied inside the agent.

### Thesis use
Version GridWorld map families and environment-generation seeds as part of the benchmark definition.

### Citation
Cobbe et al. (2020), Abstract, Introduction, and Section 2.

## E2 — Generalization uses held-out levels
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2.2; Section 3.3
- **Claim:** For generalization evaluation, agents train on a finite set of generated levels and are tested on held-out levels drawn from the environment distribution.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The benchmark separates the levels used during training from those used for zero-shot testing. This makes test performance an estimate of generalization beyond memorized training trajectories rather than another measurement on the same finite set.

### Thesis use
Keep train, validation/tuning, and final test map seeds disjoint, and separate those splits from changepoint schedules.

### Citation
Cobbe et al. (2020), Sections 2.2 and 3.3.

## E3 — Large finite training sets can still be overfit
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Introduction; Section 3.1; Figure 2
- **Claim:** Deep RL agents can exhibit substantial train–test gaps even after training on many distinct levels.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The paper varies training-set size from hundreds to many thousands of levels and shows that generalization gaps can remain large. The result warns that strong training performance on a large finite collection is not sufficient evidence that an agent learned structure that transfers to unseen levels.

### Thesis use
Retain held-out map evaluation even if the training generator produces many layouts.

### Citation
Cobbe et al. (2020), Section 3.1 and Figure 2.

## E4 — Procedural generation does not guarantee solvability
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 2.1, Level Solvability
- **Claim:** Procgen aims to generate solvable levels but does not provide an absolute solvability guarantee.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase
The authors state that their generators strive to make all levels solvable and estimate that more than 99% are solvable, while acknowledging that solvability is not strictly guaranteed.

### Thesis use
Run explicit reachability/solvability validation on every generated or structurally perturbed GridWorld used in an experiment.

### Citation
Cobbe et al. (2020), Section 2.1.

## E5 — Fixed-sequence competence can create an illusion of progress
- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 3.2; Figure 3
- **Claim:** Agents trained on a deterministic level sequence can become competent on the early training levels while performing poorly when the sequence is randomized at test time.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Use held-out randomized layouts and avoid interpreting repeated success on a fixed route or fixed layout sequence as generalization.

### Citation
Cobbe et al. (2020), Section 3.2 and Figure 3.

## E6 — Zero-shot generalization is not online recovery
- **Type:** scope synthesis grounded in the benchmark
- **Location:** Overall generalization protocol
- **Claim:** Procgen evaluates performance on unseen levels after training; it does not introduce a within-run unknown changepoint and then measure detection and relearning.
- **Status:** verified

### Thesis use
Run frozen zero-shot testing separately from any later online adaptation phase.

### Citation
Cobbe et al. (2020), overall protocol.

## `SRC-660560956D` — https---proceedings.mlr.press-v199-steinparz22a-steinparz22a.pdf

- **Συγγραφείς:** —
- **Έτος:** 2004
- **Τύπος:** ακαδημαϊκή εργασία
- **Θέματα / tags:** ανθεκτικότητα και ανάκαμψη; μη στασιμότητα; GridWorld; μερική παρατηρησιμότητα; πινακοποιημένη ενισχυτική μάθηση; βαθιά ενισχυτική μάθηση; συνεχής προσαρμογή; στατιστική αξιολόγηση
- **Ημερομηνία ελέγχου:** 2026-08-01
- **Αρχεία:** [πλήρες Markdown](sources/SRC-660560956D.md) · [αποσπάσματα](evidence/SRC-660560956D.md)
- **Εξωτερική πηγή:** [άνοιγμα](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v199/steinparz22a/steinparz22a.pdf)

## E1 — Reward, observation, and dynamics changes are distinct sources of non-stationarity

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 1 and 3; Figure 1
- **Claim:** The paper treats changes to rewards, observations, and transition dynamics as different non-stationary mechanisms with different adaptation challenges.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Steinparz et al. distinguish lifelong-RL shifts in the reward function, observation distribution, and environment dynamics. Their examples show that these mechanisms change different parts of the agent–environment interaction and can require different responses.

### Thesis use

Keep reward shifts, observation corruption/representation shifts, and transition/rule changes as separate scenario families before testing combined perturbations.

### Citation

Steinparz et al. (2022), Sections 1 and 3, Figure 1.

## E2 — Abruptness and change frequency are independent experimental axes

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 5.1
- **Claim:** The reward-shift experiments vary whether changes are abrupt or gradual and also vary the interval between repeated changes.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The study repeatedly changes the reward function under both abrupt and gradual schedules and uses different intervals between changes. This separates how quickly a regime changes from how often the agent encounters another change.

### Context and limits

The paper's numerical step counts are tied to its own training horizon and should not be copied directly into a smaller benchmark.

### Thesis use

Use a factorial design such as `change_mode × change_interval × severity`, scaling intervals relative to the learning horizon.

### Citation

Steinparz et al. (2022), Section 5.1.

## E3 — Exploration decay and stale replay can impede adaptation

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Sections 5.2–5.3; Figures 3–4
- **Claim:** In the paper's DQN experiments, poor recovery is linked to diminished exploration and replay data from the previous regime.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Vanilla DQN adapts poorly after the studied reward shifts. The authors show that changing replay-buffer management and resetting exploration at known changepoints can substantially improve adaptation, which indicates that stale experience and low post-change exploration contribute to the observed failure.

### Context and limits

The exploration reset uses prior knowledge of when changes occur and is therefore an oracle-assisted intervention.

### Thesis use

Include simple recency-aware and exploration-reset baselines and label oracle changepoint information explicitly.

### Citation

Steinparz et al. (2022), Sections 5.2–5.3, Figures 3–4.

## E4 — Recall of an old context is not evidence of adaptation to the intervening context

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 5.4; Figure 5
- **Claim:** An agent can fail in a changed context and then immediately regain performance when the original context returns, demonstrating retention without successful adaptation.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

In the Color-Swap experiment, PPO performs poorly after the observation shift but quickly returns to its previous behavior when the original environment representation is restored. The result indicates that knowledge of the original context was retained even though the policy did not successfully adapt to the changed one.

### Thesis use

Report novel-context adaptation and known-context recall as separate capabilities.

### Citation

Steinparz et al. (2022), Section 5.4, Figure 5.

## E5 — Prediction error can be change-sensitive without being a calibrated detector

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 5.5; Figures 5–6
- **Claim:** Reward-model and forward-model errors can rise when the corresponding environment component changes and can be used to increase exploration.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

Reactive Exploration uses prediction losses as intrinsic signals: reward-model error responds to changed reward contingencies and forward-model error can respond to altered observations or dynamics. Adding these signals to exploration improves adaptation in several studied shifts.

### Context and limits

Prediction error can also rise because of stochasticity, model misspecification, or optimization error. It is therefore not a calibrated changepoint probability by itself.

### Thesis use

Treat prediction error as a candidate change score and measure false triggers before using it to control exploration.

### Citation

Steinparz et al. (2022), Section 5.5, Figures 5–6.

## E6 — Adaptation strength has its own utility trade-off

- **Type:** faithful paraphrase
- **Location:** Section 5.5; Appendix B.1.2
- **Claim:** Too little intrinsic-reward weight gives insufficient reactive exploration, while too much can dominate the extrinsic task objective.
- **Status:** verified

### Faithful paraphrase

The paper's sensitivity analysis shows that the intrinsic signal must be balanced against task reward. Weak weighting may not change exploration enough after a shift, whereas strong weighting can make novelty-seeking overwhelm the extrinsic objective.

### Thesis use

Report nominal utility and sensitivity to adaptation strength, and tune this parameter only on validation regimes.

### Citation

Steinparz et al. (2022), Section 5.5 and Appendix B.1.2.
